并排除了上面整个区间。随后还完成了整族下截断参数的消元，给出更强的分支条件和变容量端点区间。**扩大后的必要条件系统是否足以排除全部种子，以及完整 PSD 证书是否存在，仍然开放。**

主要公开证据：`b1592acfdb474277`、`399341aaa6b09bdb`、`7cd0bb4a795ee1df`、`19f1d27c0eb8a588`、`480b207356f9095f`。已导出到 `research/shared_evidence/`，无需访问工作进程的私有研究目录。

另一路尝试直接构造精确乘子。`44fb89e3e8aa8c34` 给出带完整 PSD 余项自由度的一类“初始到终点弦”证书，但也证明该特定构造族达不到所需容量。它仍限制了跨块支持和某些比例关系，所以不能推成完整证书锥不存在。

## 5. 正面构造路线：有限步成功与递归障碍

已有实际 15 步、31 步的精确有理耦合构造草稿，包含所有插值约束和真实初始距离。它们尚未经过新的验证提交，也未给出原问题要求的统一渐近下界。

对固定子块、固定对齐规则的继续拼接，已经找到原始查询约束的障碍。进一步开放函数值、终点和部分几何自由度后，仍有闭环约束排除指定模型；混合叶子族也出现明确的累积损失。所有这些都是**有范围的构造族障碍**，不能说一般 W_n 也受同样上界约束。

因此最新方向是允许两个非空子块有独立尺度和相对平移，同时严格保留真实桥接关系、同一个全局函数、公共极小点和初始距离代价。

暂停前该模型完成两个固定校准，并做了一次 63 步诊断。后者返回 `user_limit`，其保存迭代点仍违反原始插值和半正定约束。因此**既不是可行构造，也不是不可行性证明**。此方向仍需精确的非空拼接实例或作用于放宽后模型的合法分离证书。

延后但没有被整体否定的路线还有时间顺序模态核方法，以及更一般的相关性/字符构造。已失败的是其中有明确假设的子族，不是所有概率或代数方法。

## 6. 计算、验证和暂停的实际状态

实际使用的是本地 Docker 中的 Danus；四个 Codex 后端均核对为 `gpt-6-astra / max`，验证服务也采用该配置。当前四个工作进程已停止，Goal 为 `paused`，没有自动恢复安排。

验证只对选定的完整辅助命题进行。C78 的独立精确重放为一次，记录时间约 5.59 秒，随后一次正式提交获接受；验证服务自己的重放没有单列时间，不能混入已测计算总数。

发现阶段的“计费式调用”累计为 854，其中包含校准、固定重放和保守补记的手工候选，不等同于 854 次求解器调用。这一类别已记录的实测时间约 280.71 秒；其他已关闭计算类别分别保留独立账本，模型推理用量不包含在这些秒数中。

必须保留的一次执行偏差：旧四步搜索原定最多 96 个不同非基线候选，审计后实际为 120 个，超出 24 个。已披露并关闭该批搜索，没有追认扩大上限，也没有把它改写成合规执行。

最新仿射拼接预算原为 4 次固定诊断、60 秒，目前用了 3 次、约 35.48 秒；暂停时剩余 1 次一并冻结。它不会因重启自动重置，任何恢复都须先读原账本。没有开放的新验证额度。

## 7. 下次接续顺序

1. 先读 `CURRENT_RUN.md`、本文、原 `PROBLEM.md` 和已接受事实 `04219554e41d9750`，再核对暂停状态及各计算账本。
2. 四步路线优先处理扩大后的截断/曲率条件系统，或者构造真正释放跨块支持限制的精确完整证书；不要再尝试给已被合法函数排除的 h(t) 制作 PSD 证书。
3. 几何路线从已保存的仿射模型和原始诊断结果接续。`user_limit` 不是正负结论；剩余额度不自动恢复或增加。
4. 若出现实际完整候选，先由另一研究进程针对精确对象攻击，再选一次目标相关验证。不要把大量辅助引理重复送去验证。
5. 如果四步种子最终不可行，保留更一般负面路线和正面路线；四步失败不能决定原问题。

The positive-value relay has an explicit nonstationary bounded partial-product family (366067681df90ad9). A separate candidate gives a sharp repeated-small-block boundedness threshold (116539ec76b9ab76). These are concrete restricted-class calculations, not the arbitrary-schedule constant theorem. Source scope is recorded in SOURCE_NOTES.md and the intake lead packet. After the actual eleven-step primitive counterexample, the residual and general scalar interfaces are again the current assignments.

The direct reset-doubling family has a complete unverified exclusion against the exact DP (29ee3816ba260cf9). The tested universal ternary primitive rule (2599c72fdf8542a0) and scalar positive-deficit recursion (79da55e26c4a7406) are also restricted failures. These do not refute the negative possibility.

The all-schedule comparison cannot be inferred from high-dimensional examples with persistent or exploding error. Finite counterexamples to proposed local inequalities should eliminate those inequalities, not the full goal. A legal fixed scalar function and exact interval recursion are stronger than a forgotten-history local state, but still require one fixed global derivative and its area.

## Fixed target and completion boundary

Original Goal ACTIVE/UNRESOLVED. Prove W_n^*/U_n^ZJ ->1 uniformly over ALL
sufficiently large horizons and ALL predetermined h>=0, or give a rigorous
fixed-relative asymptotic separation with full-class upper certificates.
W_n(h)=sup2(f_n-f_star)/(L||x0-x_star||^2), W_n^*=inf_h W_n(h), W=2R.
GD is x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t)/L. Keep every finite dimension, convex
L-smooth functions with a minimizer, actual last-iterate value, true
distance, zero steps and unbounded steps.

P0=B0=1,
F(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2,
G(x,d)=2x+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8xd)/2,
P_n=max_(k+l=n-1)F(P_k,P_l),
B_n=max_(k+l=n-1)G(P_k,B_l), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.
lambda=1+sqrt2,rho=log2(lambda); do not substitute decimal0.4239.

# Source normalization note — 2026-09-26

This is a bibliographic reconciliation, not a newly accepted project fact.

In [Ye–Liu v2](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2), equation (1) divides by (L/2)||x_0-x_*||^2, so its R_n equals this project's W_n. The attachment uses a different R convention. Its W definition and target (P)/(Q) remain unchanged.

Lemma 2.2, equation (6), states prefactor 1/[4(1+s_tail)]. However, immediately after equation (12), the constructed gap is D^2/[4(1+s_tail)], the initial distance is 1, and the paper explicitly drops a factor 2 when stating the lemma. The stronger project checkpoint coefficient 1/[2(1+s_tail)] is supported by that pre-weakening construction when all its hypotheses are matched. Cite that calculation rather than the literal lemma statement.

Theorem 1.1's displayed normalization should not be mechanically doubled. Keep explicit constants separate from the existential exponent constant.

Use one-based H_t=h_{t-1} when importing the paper's checkpoints.

## Additional literature scope, checked 2026-09-26

[Liu–Chen–Jiang–Wang, arXiv:2609.11788v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11788v1), Remark 1.2, limits optimality to recursively generated classes. Sections 2.1–2.2 explicitly distinguish ambient primitive and s-composable certificate classes despite their treewise scalar correspondence. Its phase analysis is background for the benchmark, not an all-schedule minimax proof. Any use of its asymmetric phase for this exact benchmark requires matching the recurrences and normalization.

[Zhao, arXiv:2609.11784v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11784v1) proves s-composability for a particular balanced constant-step family. Its stated certificate and sum-product hypotheses must be matched before reuse; it does not assert unrestricted schedule optimality.

Neither paper has been imported as a new Danus fact. The conditional seed-amplification candidate in research/max4_primitive_seed_amplification.md includes its needed scalar argument and leaves the required seed hypothesis unmet.

## Certificate-memory methods, screened 2026-09-26

September27 duality follow-up: SOURCE_DUAL_ARC_LEAD_20260927.md records primary checks of Shu--Wang2609.03281v1 (full slack congruence plus separate signs; tridiagonal reduced-certificate sufficient case) and Yoon--Grimmer2605.02231v2 Theorem4 (noncrossing arc characterization for optimal vertex fixed-point algorithms). The latter concerns nonexpansive residual minimax, not this GD intrinsic cone. Its dual multiplier graphs are distinct from the primal coordinate-flow graphs in C35/C39. Neither source proves general primitive reversal or the original constant-profile target.

September27 supplementary screen: intake SOURCE_LEADS_20260927_0020.md records primary-source pointers and explicit applicability limits for Liu--Ye arXiv:2609.26873v1 (strong-convex repair/bridge construction) and Bok--Altschuler's proximal silver analysis (Section3.2, Laplacian Schur-complement slack). These are optional mechanism leads, not constant-profile results or new facts. Strong-convex transfer losses, composite-operator hypotheses, opposite interpolation sign and the absence of a general re-selection theorem must all be retained.

The intake HISTORICAL_CERTIFICATE_MEMORY_LEAD.md records a bounded primary-source screen of arXiv:2207.01363, arXiv:1705.03615 and arXiv:1812.02397, with exact section pointers and scope limits. These are methodology leads for retained boundary information, not imported facts or posted resolutions of this benchmark. In particular, no conjectural completeness claim or unproved reset of an inherited filter state may be used in the project's proof.

  Hsub_n: Actual word obtained by replacing every primitive empty leaf of a fully expanded maximizing B_n tree by the specified seed, recomputing all bridges, and leaving its dominant empty leaf untouched.
  J_(h,u): Dominant energy||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D f_z-||x_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2, for weights u summing to D=1+2sum h.
  L3071: The certified lower endpoint numerator77954524439662110401113475160 for B_3071 on denominatorD80.
  L_flow: diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, where e is the all-ones column on the seed gradients.
  Lambda2up: The upper endpoint numerator7046136038847924384392699 for lambda^2 on denominatorD80.
  Lambda_ij: Nonnegative finite ordered interpolation multiplier in the primitive seed certificate, with zero diagonal.
  M_prim: The entire primitive seed certificate matrix D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2, with T the unit-diagonal prefix matrix.
  R_N: Adjacent primitive leaf-capacity ratio U_(N+1)/U_N.
  R_v: Weighted right-child gradient sum sum_i v_i g_i in the dominant composition expansion.
  T0: Map r to(1+r+sqrt(r^2+6r+1))/(2lambda), for the even adjacent primitive ratio.
  T1: Map r to lambda*(sqrt(r^2+6r+1)-r-1)/2, for the odd adjacent primitive ratio.
  T_mass: A+C+d for a primitive join with child capacities A,C and d(A+C+d)=AC.
  Theta: Lambda2up*(H3840+19D80)/(D80*L3071), a strict upper bound for Z_15/Z_3.
  U_N: Leaf-count primitive capacity C_(N-1) for N>=1, extended by U_0=0 only for scalar boundary identities.
  V_N: Leaf-count dominant mass B_(N-1) for N>=1, used only in this proof.
  V_h: Primitive energy2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)+sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2 for a length-r word of sum s.
  Z_b: Positive finite limit of lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1), for each positive integer b.
  barq_n: The midpoint(rootB_n+rootB_(n+1))/2.
  c_A: sqrt(A)-1, the maximum square-root loss from leaving the dominant leaf unchanged.
  d_pp: Positive primitive bridge excess solving d_pp(A_left+A_right+d_pp)=A_left*A_right.
  delta_n: The odd-parent root-rounding defect Delta_n^2/(4barq_n).
  e_t: Nonnegative half-grid defect max(0,rootB_t/sqrt(lambda)-v_half(t/2)).
  ell_j: (1-A^(-1/2))/sqrt(B_(3*2^j-1)) in the selected finite-ray comparison.
  ell_n: c_A/sqrt(A B_n), the relative square-root loss at scalar index n.
  n_j: The input-tree node count3*2^j-1 for the selected ray.
  rootB_n: Square root of the exact benchmark denominator B_n.
  v_half: Piecewise linear interpolation of rootB_n on the nonnegative real axis.

No asymmetric or ambient-certificate-class result is imported.", "grounding": "Primary arXivv1 directly downloaded and read; Matlas queries returned unrelated results and are not claimed as matches.", "url": "https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11788v1"}]

## statement
Uniform enclosure of the original Zhang--Jiang denominator rays and a conditional full-certificate four-step seed lift.

For a nonnegative integer n and a finite nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite dimensions, all convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and at least one minimizer x_star, and all x_0!=x_star, with x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Here L>0 is arbitrary and fixed. The empty schedule is included. This is the W=2R normalization.

For a,d>=0 define Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2 and G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2. Set C_0=B_0=1 and, for every integer n>=1, C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)) and B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)). These are the original all-split benchmark recurrences, with C_n=1+S_circle(n), B_n=1+2S_bullet(n), and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2).

For every positive integer b, the positive finite limit Z_b=lim_(j->infinity)lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1) exists. For every integer j>=0,

    lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1) <= Z_b <= lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b*2^j))+3/100)^2.

The strict oriented comparison is Z_15/Z_3<19299/2500<193/25. For every fixed nonnegative integer d, lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1-d) also converges to Z_b, over indices where it is defined.

The following further assertions are conditional. Suppose that a=(a_0,a_1,a_2,a_3) consists of four nonnegative real steps with s=sum_(i=0)^3 a_i>=168/25, and put A=1+s. Suppose there exist twenty nonnegative real multipliers Lambda_ij, for distinct i,j in {0,1,2,3,4}, with Lambda_ii=0, satisfying the following FULL five-gradient certificate. Let e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T, let T_ii=1, T_it=a_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i, and define

    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2a_0,-2a_1,-2a_2,-2a_3,2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(a_0(2-a_0),a_1(2-a_1),a_2(2-a_2),a_3(2-a_3),-s(s+2)).

Assume L_flow^T e=omega and M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 is positive semidefinite on all of R^5. In particular no equality between formal gradients is imposed at zero steps, and no coordinate or interpolation-pair support is deleted.

For each n>=0 choose any fully expanded maximizing tree of the ORIGINAL B_n recurrence, with every C child expanded by the original C recurrence. Such a tree has n primitive empty leaves and one terminal dominant empty leaf. Replace each primitive empty leaf by the actual four-step word a, retain every internal node, and leave the terminal dominant leaf empty. Recompute each actual bridge as follows: for a primitive--primitive join with child capacities A_left=1+sum(left) and A_right=1+sum(right), choose d_pp>0 solving d_pp(A_left+A_right+d_pp)=A_left*A_right and concatenate (left,1+d_pp,right); for a primitive--dominant join with left capacity A_left and right mass D_right=1+2sum(right), put zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2 and concatenate (left,1+A_left/zeta,right). Denote the resulting actual schedule by Hsub_n and its mass by Dsub_n=1+2sum Hsub_n.

Then length(Hsub_n)=5n, its full all-function objective certificate gives W_(5n)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n, and

    (sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1))^2 <= Dsub_n <= A B_n.

The equality in W is attained by a one-dimensional Huber function whose initial distance to its unique minimizer is1. For every positive integer b,

    lim_(j->infinity) B_(5(b*2^j-1)) W_(5(b*2^j-1))(Hsub_(b*2^j-1)) = Z_(5b)/(A Z_b).

In particular, for EVERY integer j>=25, with n_j=3*2^j-1 and N_j=5n_j=15*2^j-5,

    W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) < (1-1/50000) U_(N_j)^ZJ.

This theorem does not assert existence of the four-step word and multipliers in its hypothesis, does not assert their impossibility, and does not resolve the original unrestricted asymptotic minimax question.

## proof
# Complete joint proof of the selected auxiliary theorem

The only accepted predecessors are0be8b54e0a33381c (benchmark definitions,
all-schedule Huber witness, square-root contraction), b98bb807d514e80d
(equivalent primitive-capacity notation and recurrence), and
1252b8823bb5a290 (necessary smooth-convex interpolation inequalities).
Every other estimate is proved here, apart from the fully stated and
matched external symmetric scalar theorem in Section2. The explicit
finite certificate and independently sufficient check are in Section8.
The four-step seed is a hypothesis, not a supplied construction.

## 1. Definitions and exact scope

Use the exact recurrences in accepted facts 0be8b54e0a33381c and
b98bb807d514e80d. Set

    C_n = 1+S_circle(n),  B_n = 1+2S_bullet(n),  C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(a,b) = (a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,
    G(a,d) = 2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,
    C_n = max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(C_k,C_l),
    B_n = max_(k+l=n-1) G(C_k,B_l).

All maxima run over nonnegative integer k,l, with EVERY original split
retained. Write lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda), s=sqrt(lambda), and
rootB_n=sqrt(B_n). In this document rootB_n is a square-root mass, not an objective
coefficient. To discuss leaf counts, also write U_N=C_(N-1) and V_N=B_(N-1)
for N>=1, and U_0=0. These are scalar leaf-count sequences; W_n(h) always denotes the original normalized worst-case functional.

The target scalar claim is

    Z_b = lim_(j->infinity) lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1),
    Z_15 < (773/100) Z_3.                                  (E5)

The proof below derives existence of these limits as well as their enclosure.
It does not need the source's qualitative objective phase theorem.

The stronger conjecture B_(2n)<=lambda B_n for all n is NOT used. The
argument allows a rigorously bounded positive defect beyond the finite base.
No support envelope or restriction on the size of a primitive prefix is assumed.

## 2. Precisely matched literature input

The only non-elementary external input is balanced optimality of the
SYMMETRIC scalar recurrence, as proved in Corollary 4.2 of:

Yu Liu, Kang Chen, Rujun Jiang, Tianyu Wang (2026), *Optimal Recursive
Composition and Dyadic Phase Laws for Gradient Descent with Predetermined
Stepsizes*, arXiv:2609.11788v1, https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11788v1 .

Its complete statement used here is: for U_0=0, U_1=1 and
U_N=max_(1<=m<N) Omega(U_m,U_(N-m)) for integers N>=2, the sequence is strictly increasing and

    U_(2a)=lambda U_a,
    U_(2a+1)=Omega(U_a,U_(a+1)),  integer a>=0.                    (2.1)

The second identity includes U_1=Omega(U_0,U_1). The source defines its
kernel K by exactly our Omega. Proposition 2.5 and identical initial values
match its U_N to C_(N-1), by induction. No assertion about equality of ambient
primitive and s-composable certificate classes is imported. In particular,
balanced splitting is NEVER assumed for the asymmetric B recurrence.

This external input is grounded in the primary paper at
https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11788v1, not in a Matlas match. An independent
fresh download confirmed the title, authors, version, Proposition2.5,
Theorem3.1 and its proof, Theorem4.1/Corollary4.2 and their proofs, and
AppendixC.2. The source uses rho for our lambda and p for our rho.
The adopted statement is solely the symmetric scalar theorem; its local
kernel and leaf indexing have been expanded and matched here.

## 3. Elementary growth and adjacent-value bounds

First, 1<rho<4/3: lambda>2 and lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2)<16. Thus s>3/2
and lambda^(3/2)<4.

For completeness, Omega(x,y)<=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho for x,y>=0.
For positive x,y, put A=x^(1/rho), C=y^(1/rho), and T=(A+C)^rho.
Let D(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. For g(z)=log D(exp z), differentiation gives

    g''(z) = rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)
             *((1+t)^rho-1-rho*t)/D(t)^2 > 0,  t=exp z.

The last bracket has value zero at t=0 and strictly positive derivative for
t>0. Hence D(t)D(1/t)>=D(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. It follows that
(T-x)(T-y)>=2xy, or T^2-(x+y)T-xy>=0. Since T>=x+y and Omega is the
positive root of that polynomial, T>=Omega(x,y). Zero arguments follow by
continuity. Induction in the full primitive recurrence gives U_N<=N^rho.
Balanced dyadic joins give U_(2^j)=lambda^j; monotonicity then yields

    N^rho/lambda <= U_N <= N^rho,  N>=1.                  (3.1)

Also G(a,d)>=Omega(a,d) for a,d>=0. Indeed, after multiplying their
difference by two, the required inequality is
3a+sqrt(d^2+8ad)>=sqrt(a^2+6ad+d^2); squaring the nonnegative sides
leaves 8a^2+2ad+6a sqrt(d^2+8ad)>=0. Induction gives V_N>=U_N.

Here is the adjacent primitive estimate derived from (2.1). Let
R_N=U_(N+1)/U_N, Delta(r)=sqrt(r^2+6r+1), and

    T0(r)=(1+r+Delta(r))/(2lambda),
    T1(r)=lambda*(Delta(r)-r-1)/2.

Then R_(2N)=T0(R_N), R_(2N+1)=T1(R_N). Both maps fix 1 and have positive
derivative at most 1/2 on [1,lambda]: their derivatives are
(1+(r+3)/Delta(r))/(2lambda) and lambda*((r+3)/Delta(r)-1)/2;
(r+3)/Delta(r) has derivative -8/Delta(r)^3. Starting at R_1=lambda,
binary induction gives

    0<R_N-1 <= (lambda-1)/2^floor(log_2 N) <= 2sqrt(2)/N.

Using (3.1), U_(N+1)-U_N<=2sqrt(2) N^(rho-1). With U_0=0 this implies
U_m-U_(m-1)<=2sqrt(2) m^(rho-1) for every m>=1.

The full dominant recurrence now gives

    0 < V_(N+1)-V_N <= 8sqrt(2) N^(rho-1).                (3.2)

For the upper bound, take a maximizing prefix m for V_(N+1). If m>=2,
compare it to the admissible split (m-1,N+1-m) for V_N. If m=1, use
G(U_0,V_N)=V_N. The derivative of G with respect to its first variable is
2+2d/sqrt(d^2+8ad)<=4. Since m<=N, the bound follows. Strict increase
follows by choosing prefix 1 in the recurrence.

Consequently, with Delta_n=rootB_(n+1)-rootB_n and barq_n=(rootB_n+rootB_(n+1))/2,

    Delta_n <= 4sqrt(2lambda) (n+1)^(rho/2-1),
    delta_n := Delta_n^2/(4barq_n)
             <= 8lambda^(3/2) (n+1)^(rho/2-2)
             < 32 n^(-4/3),  n>=1.                       (3.3)

The denominator bound used here is rootB_n>=sqrt(U_(n+1))
>=(n+1)^(rho/2)/sqrt(lambda). No concavity of rootB_n is asserted.

## 4. Exact merge inequality and contraction along maximizing trees

Let a,b,d>0, write Q=sqrt(d), u=T_b(Q), t=T_a(u), where

    T_a(v)=(v+sqrt(v^2+8a))/2,  T_a(v)^2=G(a,v^2).

If a<=d, then

    G(a,G(b,d)) < G(Omega(a,b),d).                        (4.1)

To prove this, u>Q and Q(u+Q)>2Q^2>=2a. Since t is the positive
solution of t^2-ut=2a, this implies t<u+Q. Set r=t/u and c=a+br.
The polynomial defining Omega gives

    c^2-(a+b)c-ab = ab*(t-u-Q)/u < 0.

Here r(r-1)=2a/u^2 and 2b/u^2=1-Q/u were substituted to obtain the
identity. Thus c<Omega(a,b). Finally,
t^2-Qt=2a+(u-Q)t=2(a+bt/u)=2c<2Omega(a,b), so T_Omega(a,b)(Q)>t.
Squaring proves (4.1).

Consider two successive right-continuation joins in ANY maximizing B tree.
Let their primitive leaf counts be m1,m2 and the remaining leaf count be R.
Their values are a=U_m1, b=U_m2, d=V_R. The same total leaf count permits
merging the two primitive blocks: Omega(a,b)<=U_(m1+m2). Therefore (4.1)
would strictly improve the maximizing tree if a<=d. Necessarily a>d.
By (3.1) and V_R>=U_R,

    m1^rho >= a > d >= R^rho/lambda,
    R < 2m1,
    R < (2/3)(m1+m2+R).                                  (4.2)

Thus the remaining leaf count decreases by a factor less than 2/3 every
TWO edges along a maximizing right spine. This is an optimality argument
for the exact full DP, not a restriction imposed on allowed splits.

## 5. A one-step rounding defect inequality

Extend C_-1=0. Let v_half(x) be linear interpolation of rootB_n on nonnegative
integers: v_half(n+theta)=(1-theta)rootB_n+theta rootB_(n+1), 0<=theta<=1. Define

    e_t = max(0, rootB_t/s - v_half(t/2)),  t>=0.                  (5.1)

For a maximizing split k+l=t-1, the claim is

    e_t <= e_l                              if t is even,
    e_t <= e_l+delta_floor(t/2)              if t is odd. (5.2)

Only t>4095 will require the odd formula, so delta_0 need not be bounded.

The primitive bounds from (2.1) are

    C_(2a+1)/lambda=C_a,
    C_(2a)/lambda<= (C_(a-1)+C_a)/2.                      (5.3)

For a>=1 the second follows from
Omega(x,y)<=lambda(x+y)/2, obtained using 4xy<=(x+y)^2. For a=0,
C_0/lambda<=1/2=(C_-1+C_0)/2 because lambda>2.

The exact dominant split inequality can be written without a radical:

    2C_i+rootB_j rootB_m <= rootB_m^2,  i+j=m-1.                    (5.4)

It is equivalent to T_(C_i)(rootB_j)<=rootB_m. We additionally permit the formal
boundary i=-1,j=m, where (5.4) is equality.

If t=2n and k=2a+1,l=2b, then a+b=n-1. Use (5.4) directly. If
k=2a,l=2b+1, then a+b=n-1; average (5.4) at the two splits
(a-1,b+1) and (a,b), both with parent n. Using (5.3) gives a root at
most rootB_n in either case before adding the continuation defect e_l.

For t=2n+1, set Qbar=(rootB_n+rootB_(n+1))/2. If k=2a+1,l=2b+1,
then a+b=n-1. Average the inequalities with parent/split
(n;a,b) and (n+1;a,b+1). For u=C_a and w=(rootB_b+rootB_(b+1))/2, this gives

    2u+Qbar*w-Qbar^2 <= (Delta_n^2-Delta_n*Delta_b)/4
                       <= Delta_n^2/4.

If k=2a,l=2b, then a+b=n. Average parent/split
(n;a-1,b) and (n+1;a,b). With u=(C_(a-1)+C_a)/2 and w=rootB_b,
the corresponding upper bound is Delta_n^2/4. Boundary a=0 uses the
formal equality in (5.4). In both cases w<=Qbar. The derivative of
y^2-wy-2u for y>=Qbar is at least Qbar. Its positive root is therefore
at most Qbar+Delta_n^2/(4Qbar)=Qbar+delta_n.

Finally, T_a is increasing and 1-Lipschitz in its nonnegative argument:
T_a'(v)=(1+v/sqrt(v^2+8a))/2<=1. Homogeneity and
rootB_l/s<=v_half(l/2)+e_l imply

    rootB_t/s = T_(C_k/lambda)(rootB_l/s)
           <= T_(C_k/lambda)(v_half(l/2))+e_l.

Combining this with the preceding parity cases proves (5.2).

## 6. The exact finite base and the resulting uniform error

The saved interval calculation checks the following FINITE certificate:

    e_t=0 for every integer 0<=t<=4095.                  (6.1)

The integer tests and reproducibility information are given in section 8.
Assuming this explicit finite certificate, follow a maximizing right spine
in (5.2) until its index is at most 4095. For visited indices t_i>4095,
put N_i=t_i+1 and n_i=floor(t_i/2)>=2048. By (4.2), whenever i and i+2
are both visited, N_i>(3/2)N_(i+2). Consequently

    n_i >= (N_i-2)/2 > (3/4)N_(i+2)-1
        >= (3/2)n_(i+2)-1/4 > (4/3)n_(i+2).

The last step uses n_(i+2)>=2048. Split the visited nodes into the two
parities of their spine position. Applying (3.3) backward from the last
node of each parity yields

    e_t <= sum_i delta_(n_i)
        < 64*2048^(-4/3)/(1-(3/4)^(4/3))
        < 256*2048^(-4/3)
        < 1/100.                                        (6.2)

For the final exact comparison, 2048^4=17592186044416 exceeds
25600^3=16777216000000. Thus (6.2) holds at every t, with (6.1) handling
the finite base. In particular,

    rootB_(2n) <= s*(rootB_n+1/100) for every n>=0.              (6.3)

This is the required uniform estimate. The unproved zero-defect shifted
doubling conjecture has been replaced by an explicit permitted defect.

## 7. Uniform ray enclosure and the oriented comparison

Directly from the original recurrences,

    C_(2n+1)>=lambda C_n,  B_(2n+1)>=lambda B_n.         (7.1)

The primitive bound follows from the balanced join of two copies. For the
dominant bound, B_1=4>=lambda B_0. Induct on n and double both child leaf
counts in any maximizing split of B_n; homogeneity of G and the previous
primitive/dominant lower bounds give (7.1). Therefore the sequence
lambda^(-j)B_(b2^j-1) is increasing for every integer b>=1.

Iterating (6.3) gives

    s^(-r)rootB_(2^r N)
      <= rootB_N+(1/100)*sum_(i=0)^(r-1)s^(-i)
      < rootB_N+3/100,                                     (7.2)

because s>3/2. Monotonicity B_(2^r N-1)<=B_(2^r N) bounds each increasing
ray, proving existence of Z_b without invoking a qualitative phase limit.
Applying (7.2) at N=b2^j yields the uniform enclosure

    lambda^(-j)B_(b2^j-1)
      <= Z_b
      <= lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b2^j))+3/100)^2             (7.3)

for EVERY integer b>=1 and j>=0.

Choose upper ray b=15,j=8 (so the upper finite index is 3840) and lower
ray b=3,j=10 (finite index 3071). These two depths are the upper and lower endpoint depths for the proved enclosure.
The finite certificate shows B_3840<90000, hence

    (sqrt(B_3840)+3/100)^2 < B_3840+19.

It also gives the exact strict inequality

    lambda^2*(B_3840+19) < (773/100)*B_3071.             (7.4)

Since 10-8=2, (7.3) and (7.4) establish the proposed E5 inequality,

    Z_15/Z_3 < lambda^2*(B_3840+19)/B_3071 < 773/100.    (7.5)

The computed rational upper quotient in (7.5) is approximately
7.719573399222938; the displayed exact integer slack in section 8 is the
certificate, not this decimal. In fact the same endpoints certify the
slightly stronger comparison

    Z_15/Z_3 < 193/25 = 7.72.                            (7.6)

## 8. Explicit finite certificate and its independent exact check

The denominator is D80=2^80=1208925819614629174706176. Four arrays
pl,pu,bl,bu enclose respectively C_n from below/above and B_n from
below/above. Their index-zero values are all D80. At every n from 1
through 4095 they use EVERY original split k+l=n-1, k,l>=0.

For positive integer numerators a,b, the primitive radical numerator is
R=(a+b)^2+4ab with linear numerator c=a+b. The dominant radical
numerator is R=b^2+8ab with c=4a+b. Thus each exact join is
(c+sqrt(R))/(2D80). A lower numerator is
floor((c+floor(sqrt(R)))/2); an upper numerator is
ceil((c+ceil(sqrt(R)))/2). These expressions bound that join because
both rounding operations have the indicated orientation. Positivity
and coordinatewise monotonicity of both kernels, followed by taking
the maximum over every split, prove enclosure by induction on n.
No argmax is assumed exact and no split is removed.

Here is a complete deterministic defining program for the finite
certificate. Python integers denote arbitrary-precision integers;
isqrt(v) is the unique nonnegative integer r with r^2<=v<(r+1)^2.
The program's finite loop bounds and all asserted integer values are
part of the certificate. No floating-point quantity enters it.

```python
from math import isqrt
D80 = 1 << 80
nmax = 4095
def enclosed_join(a,b,dominant,upper):
    rad = b*b+8*a*b if dominant else (a+b)*(a+b)+4*a*b
    c = 4*a+b if dominant else a+b
    r = isqrt(rad)
    if upper:
        r += (r*r != rad)
        return (c+r+1)//2
    return (c+r)//2
pl=[D80]; pu=[D80]; bl=[D80]; bu=[D80]
for n in range(1,nmax+1):
    pl.append(max(enclosed_join(pl[k],pl[n-1-k],False,False) for k in range(n)))
    pu.append(max(enclosed_join(pu[k],pu[n-1-k],False,True) for k in range(n)))
    bl.append(max(enclosed_join(pl[k],bl[n-1-k],True,False) for k in range(n)))
    bu.append(max(enclosed_join(pu[k],bu[n-1-k],True,True) for k in range(n)))
qlo=[]; qhi=[]
for n in range(nmax+1):
    lo=isqrt(D80*bl[n]); rf=isqrt(D80*bu[n])
    assert lo*lo <= D80*bl[n] < (lo+1)**2
    assert rf*rf <= D80*bu[n] < (rf+1)**2
    qlo.append(lo); qhi.append(rf+(rf*rf != D80*bu[n]))
root2=isqrt(2*D80*D80)
assert root2*root2 <= 2*D80*D80 < (root2+1)**2
lamlo=D80+root2; lamhi=lamlo+1
slo=isqrt(D80*lamlo)
assert slo*slo <= D80*lamlo < (slo+1)**2
lam2hi=(lamhi*lamhi+D80-1)//D80
assert slo == 1878397475050142397719609
assert lam2hi == 7046136038847924384392699
slacks=[]
for t in range(nmax+1):
    n=t//2
    slack=(slo*qlo[n]-D80*qhi[t] if t%2==0 else
           slo*(qlo[n]+qlo[n+1])-2*D80*qhi[t])
    assert slack>0
    slacks.append(slack)
assert min(slacks)==33709536743225496543599832619768805273186776891
assert slacks.index(min(slacks))==4094
L3071=bl[3071]; H3840=bu[3840]
assert L3071==77954524439662110401113475160
assert H3840==103225412920462918384305104511
assert H3840<90000*D80
assert 773*L3071*D80-100*lam2hi*(H3840+19*D80)==98261575859406148591490057041393620527049740462983180
assert 772*L3071*D80-100*lam2hi*(H3840+19*D80)==4020338508518990670593580566018450094572713188395020
assert 19299*L3071*D80-2500*lam2hi*(H3840+19*D80)==6267225362087608843943037675086081931840802435287340
assert L3071-64000*D80==583271984325843219918211160
assert 2048**4>25600**3
```

In this program qlo[n]/D80<=sqrt(B_n)<=qhi[n]/D80,
slo/D80<=sqrt(lambda), and lambda^2<=lam2hi/D80. Consequently
positivity of the even and odd slacks proves, respectively,
sqrt(B_(2n))<=sqrt(lambda)*sqrt(B_n) and
sqrt(B_(2n+1))<=sqrt(lambda)*(sqrt(B_n)+sqrt(B_(n+1)))/2,
for every fine index from 0 through 4095. In the notation of Section 5,
these are exactly all 4096 inequalities e_t=0. This includes t=0 and
the final odd index4095, whose two coarse indices are2047,2048.

The full four-array table with a lower witness split for each n is
the public artifact
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA256 2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
Its identity is fixed by this hash; correctness is justified by the
enclosure induction and the following independent exact check, not
by the hash. This check does not need to assert that the table entries
are the narrowest possible enclosures or that any stored maximizing
split is a true maximizer.

For each n>=1 and every split k+l=n-1, let a=pu[k],b=pu[l],H=pu[n].
The checker verifies

    2H-a-b>=0,  (2H-a-b)^2 >= (a+b)^2+4ab.

This is exactly Omega(a/D80,b/D80)<=H/D80, by squaring two
nonnegative sides. For the dominant recurrence it puts
a=pu[k],b=bu[l],H=bu[n] and checks

    2H-4a-b>=0,  (2H-4a-b)^2 >= b^2+8ab.

These are sufficient upper bounds for ALL original splits. For each
lower endpoint it checks a single stored admissible split with the
lower child endpoints. If L is the parent lower endpoint numerator
and c,R are the corresponding linear/radical numerators, it verifies
either 2L-c<=0 or (2L-c)^2<=R. In the first case sqrt(R)>=2L-c
because sqrt(R)>=0; in the second case the same inequality follows
by squaring. Hence the actual maximum is at least L/D80. Starting
from all four exact index-zero values, induction certifies EVERY
stored interval without any reliance on the original generator's
execution. This proves sufficiency of the independent checker.

The checker then forms new qlo,qhi using integer square roots,
checks the defining inequalities for every root, recomputes all4096
base slacks, and checks the endpoint comparisons in this section.
It retains all slacks in independent_base_slacks.csv. The new file
has SHA256 ab3ece1dc47d512fe9399b1832e649ee09e59beec3e299e85839d9dd3334d5a7.

The independent source is
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/independent_certificate_check.py,
SHA256 5c79d614a1a98617a5f978f740ef0937f3e5dd773dd9cf052b8afda90add6244.
Its complete successful result is
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/independent_certificate_result.json,
SHA256 bc8e00fffc534b8cecb12181b09bb104e865ecb935b68f4514b80ac36bce26be.
It checked 8,386,560 original upper splits for EACH sequence,
8,190 lower witnesses, and all4,096 base inequalities, completing
with exit status0 in5.589835043007042 seconds. All16 original public
manifest files matched their hashes. The old phase-discovery ledger
was not modified. This is one independently checked finite certificate,
not a discovery scan and not an assumption of zero defect at larger
indices.

## 9. A sharper rational endpoint comparison and a direct convergence rate

Define the following fixed positive integers from the complete certificate in Section8:

    D80 = 1208925819614629174706176,
    L3071 = 77954524439662110401113475160,
    H3840 = 103225412920462918384305104511,
    Lambda2up = 7046136038847924384392699.

They have the enclosing meanings

    L3071/D80 <= B_3071,
    B_3840 <= H3840/D80,
    lambda^2 <= Lambda2up/D80.

Set

    Theta = Lambda2up*(H3840+19D80)/(D80*L3071).

The two exact integer comparisons needed below are

    19299*L3071*D80-2500*Lambda2up*(H3840+19D80)
      = 6267225362087608843943037675086081931840802435287340 > 0,
    L3071-64000D80 = 583271984325843219918211160 > 0.

Thus

    Theta < 19299/2500 = 193/25-1/2500,
    B_3071 > 64000.                                      (9.1)

These are two fixed integer checks using the saved endpoints, not another
recurrence computation. They are included in the complete finite certificate in Section8. Section 7 now gives

    Z_15/Z_3 < lambda^2*(B_3840+19)/B_3071
              <= Theta < 19299/2500.                     (9.2)

Here the initial strict inequality follows from B_3840<90000 and the
strict bound (sqrt(B_3840)+3/100)^2<B_3840+19. Both positive ray limits
exist by Section 7. No theorem about a qualitative objective phase or its
nonconstancy enters this argument.

An elementary global upper bound makes the convergence error explicit:

    B_n <= 4C_n <= 4(n+1)^rho,  n>=0.                    (9.3)

Indeed, for a,b>=0,

    G(a,4b)=2a+2b+2sqrt(b^2+2ab)
           <=2a+2b+2sqrt(a^2+6ab+b^2)=4Omega(a,b).

The initial bound B_0<=4C_0 holds. If it holds at every smaller index,
then each ORIGINAL dominant split obeys

    G(C_k,B_l)<=G(C_k,4C_l)<=4Omega(C_k,C_l)<=4C_n

when k+l=n-1. Maximizing gives the first assertion of (9.3). The second
was proved in Section 3. Balanced splitting is not assumed for B.

For every positive integer b and integer j>=0, put M=b*2^j and

    E(b,j)=8sqrt(2)*b^(rho-1)*2^(-j)
           +(3/25)*(b+1)^(rho/2)*lambda^(-j/2)
           +(9/10000)*lambda^(-j).

Then the enclosure in (7.3) implies the explicit bound

    0 <= Z_b-lambda^(-j)B_(M-1) <= E(b,j).               (9.4)

To prove it, subtract the lower endpoint in (7.3) from its upper endpoint:

    Z_b-lambda^(-j)B_(M-1)
      <=lambda^(-j)[B_M-B_(M-1)+(3/50)sqrt(B_M)+9/10000].

The adjacent bound (3.2) gives B_M-B_(M-1)<=8sqrt(2)M^(rho-1).
By (9.3), sqrt(B_M)<=2(M+1)^(rho/2). Substitution, lambda=2^rho,
and b+2^(-j)<=b+1 give exactly (9.4).

If d is a fixed nonnegative integer and M-1-d>=0, monotonicity and the
same adjacent bound give

    0 <= B_(M-1)-B_(M-1-d) <= 8sqrt(2)*d*M^(rho-1).

Every increment on the left has its index at most M-1, which justifies
using the displayed common upper bound. Consequently

    0 <= Z_b-lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1-d)
      <= E(b,j)+8sqrt(2)*d*b^(rho-1)*2^(-j).             (9.5)

In particular this shifted sequence converges to the SAME Z_b. This
supplies the precise bounded-offset step needed for actual substitution
without importing the source objective-phase theorem. For b ranging over
a finite set and d bounded by a fixed integer, the explicit bound is
uniform. No conclusion that the error is zero at a finite index is made.

The next three sections prove the full original-function seed and composition identities within this proof.

## 10. Full original-space seed condition

For a nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) denotes the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over every finite-dimensional
Euclidean space, every convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient
and a minimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star, under
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Here L>0, and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Normalize L=1, translate a selected minimizer to zero, and subtract f_star
so that its value is also zero; the optimum jet is x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For a finite
nonnegative word h of length r, let s=sum h_i, A=1+s, and retain every
formal gradient g_0,...,g_r, including gradients at zero steps. Put

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
          +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2.

Every actual smooth convex trajectory has Q_ij<=0 by accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraph1, with exactly this residual orientation. We call the seed
primitive when V_h>=0 on every such trajectory in every finite dimension.
A concrete sufficient certificate retains ALL ordered finite multipliers
Lambda_ij>=0, i!=j, and Lambda_ii=0. Let e be the all-ones column, and set

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).

For a real matrix K, sym(K)=(K+K^T)/2. Gram(g_0,...,g_r) denotes the matrix of all pairwise Euclidean inner products of these formal gradients, and <M,Gram> means trace(M^T Gram).

The feasibility conditions are

    L_flow^T e=omega,
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0.            (4)

Indeed substituting the GD equations and collecting function coefficients
and gradient inner products gives the exact identity

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.

To audit the matrix formula, the position terms of sum Lambda Q contribute
sym(L_flow(T-I)); the squared-difference terms contribute
sym(L_flow)-diag(omega)/2. Adding V_h's diagonal yields (4).
Thus (4) proves the required actual primitive inequality. It has no support,
rank, symmetry, quotient, or terminal-edge restriction.

Adding optimum multipliers cannot supply a missing positive x_0 diagonal:
that diagonal is zero. Its mixed coefficient with g_i is
-Lambda_(i,star), so PSD forces each Lambda_(i,star)=0. Summing the finite
function-flow equations then forces sum_i Lambda_(star,i)=0, and their
nonnegativity makes all these multipliers zero. Therefore the original
formal space (x_0,g_0,...,g_r) is retained, with its x_0 row zero.
No equality between formal gradients at duplicated query positions is
imposed. For the implication used here, sufficiency of (4) is enough;
no unproved dual attainment is invoked.

## 11. Complete primitive and dominant composition identities

Primitive join: let a,b be primitive, with capacities A=1+sum a and
C=1+sum b. Choose d>0 by d(A+C+d)=AC, put T_mass=A+C+d and
kappa=(T_mass/C)^2, and use the physical word h=(a,1+d,b).
Let p be its bridge query, z its terminal query, and j range over the
nonterminal queries of b. Direct expansion gives

    V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1) E_PP,
    E_PP=sum_j b_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)+d Q_(p,z)
                                      +sum_j b_j Q_(j,z).       (5)

For a coefficient audit, the value part of E_PP is
2sum b_j f_j+(d-C)f_p+(2-C-d)f_z. Its gradient part is

    sum_j b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2
       +[(C-d)/2-d^2]||g_p||^2+(C+d)||g_z||^2/2.

The remaining coefficient identities are
(kappa-1)(C-d)=2(A+d), kappa d^2=A^2, and kappa C^2=T_mass^2.
They follow from d T_mass=AC. Also 0<d<C and kappa>1, so all
added multipliers are nonnegative. If the child certificates have arbitrary
PSD remainders, the full parent remainder is their original-coordinate sum
M_a+kappa M_b, embedded on the actual child-gradient indices. The resulting
capacity is exactly Omega(A,C).
The physical bridge obeys 1+d=phi(A-1,C-1), as required by the recurrences in Section 1.

Dominant join: for a word h with terminal z, put D=2sum h+1. For
nonnegative weights u_i summing to D define

    J_(h,u)=||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D f_z
                                  -||x_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.    (6)

The empty word has u=1 and J=0. A primitive word is dominant with
u=(h,s+1), since direct expansion gives J=V_h. In general J>=0 implies
2f_z<=||x_0||^2/D on every actual trajectory.

Let a be a primitive left child of capacity A and b a dominant right child
with mass D and weights v. Define

    mu=2A/D,  zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2,
    gamma=A+1,  beta=1+A/zeta.

Use the physical parent word (a,beta,b), and weights u=(a,gamma,zeta v).
Their mass is G(A,D)=D zeta^2=2A+zeta D=2sum(parent)+1. With p the
bridge query, all right-child queries i>p, including its terminal, give

    J_parent=V_a+zeta^2 J_right
                         -2mu sum_(i>p)v_i Q_(i,p).             (7)

The physical bridge is exactly beta=psi(A-1,(D-1)/2); multiply
zeta^2-zeta=2A/D by D/zeta to check this against the radical in psi.

This is an equality on the full original formal space. Function
coefficients use mu D=2A. Expanding all squares and the GD bridge uses

    (A-1)A+mu D+gamma=gamma^2,
    zeta^2=zeta+mu,  zeta^2 beta=mu+gamma zeta;

these cancel the mixed terms and give (7). The child remainder is pulled
back from the actual right-child starting position x_p-beta g_p; hence the
original x_0 and all inherited gradients remain in the certificate.
For an additional coefficient check, put y=x_p-beta g_p,
xi_i=x_i-y on the right child, and R_v=sum_(i>p)v_i g_i. Expanding
J_parent-V_a-zeta^2 J_right gives precisely

    -2mu sum_i v_i(f_i-f_p)
    -mu sum_i v_i||g_i||^2-mu D||g_p||^2
    +2mu sum_i v_i<g_i,xi_i>-2mu(beta-1)<R_v,g_p>.

Indeed the left-prefix displacement cancels between the position terms
and the final square; its remaining diagonal is V_a's diagonal. The
bridge diagonal difference is -2A=-mu D. The right-child local quadratic
has coefficient zeta-zeta^2=-mu, and the remaining bridge cross coefficient
is 2zeta(beta-gamma)=-2mu(beta-1). As

    Q_(i,p)=f_i-f_p+(beta-1)<g_i,g_p>-<g_i,xi_i>
                         +(||g_i||^2+||g_p||^2)/2,

the displayed expansion is exactly the last term in (7). This also checks
the endpoint i=z and the empty-right-child case without deleting either.
All extra interpolation coefficients are nonnegative. Induction on the
actual tree supplies a full PSD objective certificate: divide (6), after
inserting its nonnegative interpolation representation, by D. Its PSD
remainder is the retained final square plus the embedded child remainders,
all divided by D. This is an all-function objective bound, not a bound on
a quadratic, projection or temporal subclass.

## 12. The exact physical substitution and the untouched dominant leaf

Fix an ACTUAL seed a satisfying Section 10, of length r=m-1 and capacity
A=1+sum a>=1. For each n choose a fully expanded maximizing dominant tree
for B_n in Section 1, for example the smallest maximizing split at each node.
The unique dominant right spine ends at one dominant empty leaf. Every
other leaf is primitive: there are exactly n of them, by induction on those scalar recurrences.

Replace each primitive empty leaf by the actual word a, retaining every
original node, and recompute each physical bridge by (5) or (7). Leave the
last dominant empty leaf empty, with mass 1. Call the resulting word Hsub_n
and its true mass Dsub_n=2sum Hsub_n+1. There are n old bridge steps and nr
inserted seed steps. Thus

    length(Hsub_n)=mn,  W_(mn)(Hsub_n)<=1/Dsub_n.                       (8)

Every primitive branch capacity is multiplied by A, by homogeneity of
Omega. If the last dominant leaf were ALSO changed from mass 1 to A in
an auxiliary scalar evaluation, homogeneity of G would make the root
A B_n. This last change is scalar only, not an additional physical block.
The square-root join is

    sqrt(G(a,d))=(sqrt(d)+sqrt(d+8a))/2.

Fact 0be8b54e0a33381c proves that it is increasing and 1-Lipschitz in
sqrt(d). Propagating the final-leaf difference along the whole spine gives

    sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1)<=sqrt(Dsub_n)<=sqrt(A B_n).            (9)

Writing c_A=sqrt(A)-1 and ell_n=c_A/sqrt(A B_n), this implies

    (1-ell_n)^2<=Dsub_n/(A B_n)<=1,
    0<=A B_n-Dsub_n<=2c_A sqrt(A B_n)-c_A^2.                    (10)

The lower square-root bound is positive because B_n>=1. The accepted
bound B_n>=2n+1 already makes ell_n tend to zero; the elementary lower bound
B_n>=C_n>=(n+1)^rho/lambda in Section 3 also gives
ell_n=O(n^(-rho/2)). The bound is independent of tree depth,
split ties and multiplier choices.

In fact (8) is equality. The actual nonnegative word Hsub_n has sum
(Dsub_n-1)/2. The one-dimensional Huber function in fact 0be8b54e0a33381c,
started at x_0=1 with threshold 1/Dsub_n and minimizer 0, attains normalized
error 1/Dsub_n on that entire word. Its initial distance is genuinely 1.
Together with the full upper certificate this proves, jointly,

    W_(mn)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n.                                      (11)

Restoring any L>0 by multiplying the normalized objective by L leaves
the specified h_i/L GD updates and the ratio 2gap/(L distance^2) unchanged.

## 13. The explicit selected-ray consequence, including a finite horizon

All assertions in this section are CONDITIONAL on an actual primitive
seed as specified in Section 10. The original problem requires existence
or a different unconditional route, and no such seed is supplied here.

Fix a nonnegative primitive seed of length r=m-1 and capacity A. Write
Hsub_n and Dsub_n for the actual substituted word and its mass from Section 12.
The full certificate and Huber witness in that section give

    W_(mn)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n,
    (sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1))^2 <= Dsub_n <= A B_n.          (13.1)

For every fixed positive integer b, let n_j=b*2^j-1. The true output
horizon is mn_j=mb*2^j-m. In (9.5), use mb in place of b and d=m-1
to obtain

    lambda^(-j)B_(mn_j) -> Z_(mb).                       (13.2)

Meanwhile (9.3) and (13.1) imply

    0<=lambda^(-j)(A B_(n_j)-Dsub_(n_j))
      <=4(sqrt(A)-1)sqrt(A)*b^(rho/2)*lambda^(-j/2).

This tends to zero. Together with (9.4) and Z_b>=B_(b-1)>0 it proves

    B_(mn_j) W_(mn_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) -> Z_(mb)/(A Z_b).        (13.3)

This is the exact comparison for each fixed integer ray, derived from
the scalar enclosure and actual unchanged final leaf. It is not an
assertion that an integer b optimizes a continuous phase. It does not
claim necessity for all possible subsequences or all possible seed uses.
It proves the selected-ray implication with no qualitative phase input.

For m=5 and b=3 the comparison is stronger than an eventual limit claim.
Let n_j=3*2^j-1 and N_j=5n_j=15*2^j-5. For j>=10, the exact dyadic
lower scaling (7.1) and monotonicity toward the upper ray limit give

    B_(n_j)>=lambda^(j-10) B_3071,
    B_(N_j)<=B_(15*2^j-1)<=lambda^j Z_15
                         <lambda^(j-8)(B_3840+19).

Divide these inequalities and use (9.1):

    B_(N_j)/B_(n_j)<Theta,  j>=10.                       (13.4)

The last dominant leaf has NOT been physically replaced. Let

    ell_j=(1-A^(-1/2))/sqrt(B_(n_j)).

For A>=1 and n_j>=1 one has 0<=ell_j<1/sqrt(B_(n_j))<1. Equations
(13.1) and (13.4) yield the fully finite estimate

    B_(N_j)W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))
      < Theta/[A(1-ell_j)^2]
      <=Theta/[A(1-B_(n_j)^(-1/2))^2],  j>=10.          (13.5)

The last displayed weak inequality also covers A=1. There is no loss of
the original function certificate, of any gradient, or of the terminal
offset in this estimate.

Here is an explicit rational conditional theorem. Suppose the seed has
length four and

    A>=A0:=193/25.

For j>=25, (9.1) and the elementary inequalities lambda>2 and lambda^2>5
give

    B_(n_j)>=lambda^(j-10)B_3071
             >64000 lambda^15
             >64000*2*5^7=10000000000.

Hence B_(n_j)^(-1/2)<1/100000. Define delta=1/50000. From (9.1),

    Theta/A <=Theta/A0 <1-1/19300 <1-2delta,
    (1-B_(n_j)^(-1/2))^2
          >(1-1/100000)^2>1-delta.

Since 0<delta<1,

    (1-2delta)/(1-delta)<1-delta

by multiplication by 1-delta: the right side contributes the additional
positive term delta^2. Substitution into (13.5) proves

    W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) < (1-1/50000) U_(N_j)^ZJ
    for EVERY integer j>=25,
    n_j=3*2^j-1,  N_j=15*2^j-5.                          (13.6)

This conclusion includes A=A0. The strict scalar margin pays for the
untouched final leaf, so strict inequality A>A0 is unnecessary for this
particular sufficient interface. For any A>Theta, (13.5) likewise supplies
an explicit finite starting depth after choosing a smaller positive margin.
No near-optimality of the displayed depth 25 or margin 1/50000 is claimed.

The completely explicit missing object can therefore be stated as follows:
four nonnegative steps h_0,...,h_3 with sum at least 168/25, and all twenty
ordered finite multipliers Lambda_ij>=0 for distinct i,j in {0,...,4},
whose full five-gradient matrix in Section 10 is positive semidefinite
and whose exact finite-node flow is omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_3,2sum h_i).
The full x_0 row remains zero, as derived there. Neither existence of that
matrix and word nor impossibility is proved. No seed search was performed.

## intuition
A strict oriented comparison of two exact benchmark rays pays for the one unchanged dominant leaf when a four-step full primitive certificate is substituted at every primitive leaf. The scalar enclosure uses a finite exact base plus a summable defect along actual maximizing spines; it does not assert concavity or balanced dominant joins.

glossary_introduces:

  B_n: The exact benchmark denominator 2 S_bullet(n)+1.
  C_E: Maximum positive square-root mass improvement of a seed in E over the same-length benchmark.
  ConPD: The schedule join [a,psi(s(a),s(b)),b] defined explicitly in the statement.
  D_j: Actual mass 2s(H_j)+1.
  E: Nonempty finite library of nonnegative terminal schedules; reference scalar masses are separately subscripted E_j.
  E_j: Reference mass sequence starting at B_{m_b} and using the same G joins.
  G: Scalar mass join G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.
  H_j: Schedule obtained after j successive displayed ConPD joins from the seed H_0=b.
  N_j: Length of H_j.
  S_bullet: Exact dominant-sum dynamic-programming sequence defined in the statement.
  S_circle: Exact primitive-sum dynamic-programming sequence defined in the statement.
  T_A: Square-root mass join q maps to (q+sqrt(q^2+8A))/2.
  U_n^ZJ: The exact Zhang–Jiang benchmark 1/B_n.
  W_n: Normalized worst-case last-iterate objective error over all finite-dimensional smooth convex instances, defined in the statement.
  a_j: Nonnegative prefix schedule of length k_j and sum at most S_circle(k_j).
  f_star: The minimum objective value f(x_star).
  m_b: Length of terminal schedule b.
  phi: Primitive scalar bridge function defined in the statement.
  psi: Dominant scalar bridge function defined in the statement.
  s(h): Sum of entries of the finite schedule h, with empty sum zero.
  tau: Positive Huber threshold 1/(2s+1) for total stepsize sum s.
  x_star: A minimizer of the smooth convex objective.
external_refs: []
---

## statement
Finite terminal-block improvements wash out under the Zhang–Jiang ConPD rule.

Define phi(x,y)=(-x-y+sqrt((x+y+2)^2+4(x+1)(y+1)))/2 and psi(x,y)=(3-2y+sqrt((2y+1)(2y+8x+9)))/4 for x,y>=0. Define S_circle(0)=S_bullet(0)=0 and, for n>=1,
S_circle(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+phi(S_circle(k),S_circle(n-k-1))+S_circle(n-k-1)],
S_bullet(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+psi(S_circle(k),S_bullet(n-k-1))+S_bullet(n-k-1)].
Write B_n=2 S_bullet(n)+1 and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

For a nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}), let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite-dimensional convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient, a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, with x_{t+1}=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t)/L.

Let E be any nonempty finite set of finite-length nonnegative schedules, allowing the empty schedule. For b in E, write m_b for its length and s(b) for the sum of its entries. Define
C_E=max_{b in E} max{0,sqrt(2s(b)+1)-sqrt(B_{m_b})}.
Construct a schedule by choosing b in E and an integer r>=0, setting H_0=b, and, for j=1,...,r, choosing any nonnegative schedule a_j of length k_j>=0 with s(a_j)<=S_circle(k_j) and setting
H_j=[a_j, psi(s(a_j),s(H_{j-1})), H_{j-1}].
If N=m_b+sum_{j=1}^r(k_j+1), then every such schedule satisfies
W_N(H_r)>=1/(sqrt(B_N)+C_E)^2
=U_N^ZJ (1+C_E/sqrt(B_N))^(-2).
Moreover B_N>=2N+1. Consequently the lower-bound ratio tends to 1 uniformly over all schedules in this construction as N tends to infinity. In particular no fixed positive relative asymptotic improvement below U_N^ZJ can be obtained solely by using a fixed finite library of improved terminal blocks while retaining the displayed ConPD rule and the primitive-block sum caps. This is a statement about that construction class, not the unrestricted minimax problem.

## proof
All arguments below are elementary and self-contained; no previously verified or external theorem is invoked.

1. A lower bound valid for every nonnegative schedule.
Fix any length n>=0 and any h in [0,infinity)^n. Put s=s(h)>=0 and tau=1/(2s+1). In dimension one, for any fixed L>0 define
f(z)=L z^2/2 when |z|<=tau, and f(z)=L(tau|z|-tau^2/2) when |z|>=tau.
The two expressions and their first derivatives agree at z=+tau and z=-tau. The derivative is L times z clipped to [-tau,tau]. This derivative is nondecreasing, so f is convex. Clipping is 1-Lipschitz: if u<=v, its increment lies between 0 and v-u, as can be checked from the three intervals separated by -tau and tau. Thus f has L-Lipschitz derivative. It is differentiable everywhere, has minimizer x_star=0, and f_star=0. Take x_0=1.
Nonnegativity of h implies every partial sum lies in [0,s]. An induction on i yields x_i=1-tau sum_{t=0}^{i-1}h_t: indeed this formula implies
x_i>=1-tau s=(s+1)/(2s+1)>=tau,
so f'(x_i)=L tau and the next gradient update gives the same formula at i+1. This also handles s=0 and equality at the boundary. Thus
x_n=(s+1)/(2s+1), f(x_n)=L tau x_n-L tau^2/2=L/(2(2s+1)).
Since |x_0-x_star|^2=1, this legal instance proves W_n(h)>=1/(2s(h)+1), including n=0.

2. Scalar form of one join.
For x,y>=0 put A=x+1>=1 and D=2y+1>=1. Direct substitution into the definition of psi gives
2[x+psi(x,y)+y]+1
=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2
=((sqrt(D)+sqrt(D+8A))/2)^2.
Denote the middle expression by G(A,D) and define T_A(q)=(q+sqrt(q^2+8A))/2 for q>=0. Then sqrt(G(A,D))=T_A(sqrt(D)). Both G and T are increasing in each of their displayed arguments.
For q>=p>=0, rationalizing the difference of square roots gives
0<=T_A(q)-T_A(p)
=(q-p)/2 [1+(q+p)/(sqrt(q^2+8A)+sqrt(p^2+8A))]
<=q-p,
because the denominator in the fraction is at least q+p; when q=p=0 the difference is zero without division. It follows also by interchanging q and p that T_A is 1-Lipschitz. In particular if q<=p+C for C>=0, monotonicity and this inequality imply T_A(q)<=T_A(p)+C.
All bridge steps are nonnegative: since x,y>=0, sqrt((2y+1)(2y+8x+9))>=2y+1, the formula gives psi(x,y)>=1.

3. A reference recursion dominated by the exact benchmark.
Fix a construction from the statement. Write N_0=m_b, N_j=N_{j-1}+k_j+1 and A_j=1+s(a_j). Its actual masses D_j=2s(H_j)+1 satisfy
D_0=2s(b)+1 and D_j=G(A_j,D_{j-1}).
Set reference masses E_0=B_{m_b} and E_j=G(A_j,E_{j-1}).
We prove E_j<=B_{N_j} by induction. The assertion is equality at j=0. If it holds at j-1, then the primitive-block sum cap and monotonicity of G give
E_j<=G(1+S_circle(k_j), B_{N_{j-1}}).
In the defining maximum for S_bullet(N_j), choose the valid index k=k_j; the remaining index is N_j-k_j-1=N_{j-1}. By the identity in part 2 this choice yields
B_{N_j}>=G(1+S_circle(k_j), B_{N_{j-1}}).
This establishes the induction without any assumed structure for the a_j schedules beyond their length and sum cap.

4. Uniform comparison with a finite seed library.
By definition of C_E, sqrt(D_0)<=sqrt(E_0)+C_E. Applying the last assertion of part 2 inductively with A=A_j shows sqrt(D_j)<=sqrt(E_j)+C_E for every j. Part 3 gives
sqrt(2s(H_r)+1)=sqrt(D_r)<=sqrt(B_N)+C_E.
Apply the legal Huber lower bound in part 1:
W_N(H_r)>=1/D_r>=1/(sqrt(B_N)+C_E)^2.
This proves the claimed finite-horizon inequality.

5. Divergence and uniformity.
The maximum defining B_n includes k=0, so B_n>=G(1,B_{n-1}). Since sqrt(D^2+8D)>=D for D>=0, G(1,D)>=D+2. Starting with B_0=1 gives B_n>=2n+1 by induction.
Because E is finite, C_E is a finite constant independent of the depth r, of the chosen seed, and of all the a_j schedules. Thus
(1+C_E/sqrt(B_N))^(-2)>=(1+C_E/sqrt(2N+1))^(-2)->1.
Explicitly, for epsilon in (0,1), when C_E>0 choose any integer N_epsilon>=0 for which
sqrt(2N_epsilon+1)>=C_E/((1-epsilon)^(-1/2)-1);
if C_E=0 take N_epsilon=0. Then for every constructed H_r of any length N>=N_epsilon,
W_N(H_r)>=(1-epsilon)U_N^ZJ.
This threshold is independent of the constructed schedule. Were a fixed delta in (0,1) and an unbounded sequence of constructed schedules to satisfy W_N(H_r)<=(1-delta)U_N^ZJ, choose epsilon=delta/2 and N>=N_epsilon, obtaining a contradiction. The conclusion is restricted to the displayed construction; it imposes no assertion on arbitrary schedules outside that class.

## intuition
The ConPD operation is nonexpansive in square-root mass. A fixed finite terminal improvement is therefore only an additive bounded error on this scale, which is negligible against the growing exact benchmark. The elementary Huber lower bound turns this mass comparison into a restriction on true worst-case performance.

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\handoffs\gd-exact-profile-20261010-full-evidence.zip!/project/fact_graph/facts/0e7e78056dc6258c.md

证据标签：historical_fact_file_check_original_scope。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000185 -->
---
fact_id: 0e7e78056dc6258c
problem_id: gd-exact-profile-20260926
author: max
predecessors: []
glossary_introduces:
  F: For each sampled instance, normalized objective (f-f(x_star))/L.
  T_i: b plus the sum of q_k over i<=k<=n.
  V_n: Any positive comparison sequence such that n^2 V_n tends to infinity.
  W_n: Unrestricted normalized worst-case objective error from the project; this result does not upper-bound it.
  X: Random initial displacement x_0-x_star, with norm1 almost surely.
  a_i: Expected initial-gradient inner product E<X,g_i>.
  b: Nonnegative terminal excess p_n-q_n/2.
  beta_n: Orthogonal-moment error cap 4/(1+sqrt(1+2c_n))^2.
  c_k: Nonnegative scalar sequence c_0=0, c_{k+1}=c_k+2+2sqrt(c_k+1).
  f: A sampled convex differentiable objective whose gradient is L-Lipschitz.
  g_i: Normalized random gradient grad f(x_i)/L.
  h: The fixed finite nonnegative stepsize schedule.
  p_i: Expected normalized objective value E F(x_i).
  q_i: Expected squared normalized gradient norm E||g_i||^2.
  r: Scalar (1+sqrt(1+2c_n))/2.
  s_k: Scalar sqrt(c_k+1) used to estimate c_k.
  x_i: The iterate after i gradient-descent steps.
  x_star: Chosen minimizer in each sampled instance.
external_refs: []
---

## statement
Orthogonal-moment obstruction for randomized gradient-descent adversaries. Define c_0=0 and c_{k+1}=c_k+2+2 sqrt(c_k+1) for all integers k>=0, and define beta_n=4/(1+sqrt(1+2c_n))^2. Let n>=1 be an integer, L>0, and h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}) in [0,infinity)^n be any predetermined schedule with finite entries. Consider any probability distribution on legal instances in a common finite-dimensional Euclidean space: each realization consists of a convex differentiable f:R^d->R with L-Lipschitz gradient, a minimizer x_star, and an initial point x_0 with ||x_0-x_star||=1. For each realization let x_{i+1}=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i), 0<=i<n, and set g_i=grad f(x_i)/L, including g_n. Assume all data used in expectations are measurable and E<g_i,g_j>=0 whenever 0<=i<j<=n. Then
E[2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)] <= beta_n <= 2/n^2.
The deterministic conclusion follows by taking a point-mass distribution: every legal trajectory whose n+1 gradients are mutually orthogonal has normalized last-iterate objective error at most beta_n. Moreover n^2 beta_n tends to 2. Consequently, for any positive comparison sequence V_n with n^2 V_n tending to infinity, every family of such randomized adversaries has expected normalized error divided by V_n tending to zero, uniformly over schedules and distributions satisfying the assumptions. This bounds the strength of the orthogonal-moment adversary class; it is not an upper bound on the unrestricted worst-case error W_n(h), and it does not resolve the project's unrestricted minimax target.

## proof
All expectations below refer to the distribution in the statement. No external result is invoked without a derivation.

1. Normalize and prove the required smooth-convex inequality.
In each realization define F(x)=(f(x)-f(x_star))/L. This is convex, has 1-Lipschitz gradient, has minimum value0 at x_star, and its gradient at x_i is g_i. Its gradient at a minimizer is0: if a differentiable function has a minimum, each directional derivative there is0.
For any differentiable function H with 1-Lipschitz gradient and any vectors u,v, the fundamental theorem of calculus along the line segment gives
H(u+v)-H(u)-<grad H(u),v>
=integral_0^1 <grad H(u+tv)-grad H(u),v>dt <= ||v||^2/2.
For fixed y define H(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z>. Convexity and grad H(y)=0 imply H(y)<=H(z) for all z. Apply the preceding inequality at u=x and v=-(grad F(x)-grad F(y)). Since grad H(x)=grad F(x)-grad F(y), this gives
H(y)<=H(x)-||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.
Rearranging proves, for every x,y,
F(x)>=F(y)+<grad F(y),x-y>+||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2. (I)
All uses of smooth convex interpolation below are this necessary inequality, now proved directly.

2. Integrability and notation.
Write X=x_0-x_star, p_i=E F(x_i), q_i=E||g_i||^2, and a_i=E<X,g_i> for 0<=i<=n. The notation p_i denotes an expected value, whereas F and g_i can be random. Since ||X||=1 in every realization, E||X||^2=1.
The recursion and the Lipschitz gradient imply
||x_i-x_star||<=product_{t=0}^{i-1}(1+h_t)
and ||g_i||<=||x_i-x_star||. Empty products equal1. The line-segment bound in part1 at x_star gives 0<=F(x_i)<=||x_i-x_star||^2/2. Thus every expectation used here is finite even though the finite steps have no uniform upper bound.
If some q_i=0, nonnegativity implies g_i=0 almost surely. Then x_i is a minimizer almost surely by convexity, and all later iterates equal x_i. Therefore p_n=0, and the required inequality follows since beta_n>0. It remains to handle q_i>0 for every i.

3. Lower bounds forced on the initial displacement.
From (I) against the minimizer, p_n>=q_n/2. Set b=p_n-q_n/2>=0.
Apply (I) with x=x_i, y=x_{i+1}. The GD identity is x_i-x_{i+1}=h_i g_i. Expected orthogonality gives E<g_{i+1},g_i>=0 and E||g_i-g_{i+1}||^2=q_i+q_{i+1}. Hence
p_i-p_{i+1}>=(q_i+q_{i+1})/2
for each 0<=i<n. Summing these inequalities from i through n-1 yields
p_i>=p_n+q_i/2+sum_{k=i+1}^{n-1}q_k+q_n/2
when i<n.
Apply (I) with x=x_star and y=x_i. It gives
E<g_i,x_i-x_star>>=p_i+q_i/2.
The recursion gives x_i-x_star=X-sum_{t<i}h_t g_t. Expected orthogonality therefore gives E<g_i,x_i-x_star>=a_i. For every i, including i=n, we conclude
a_i >= T_i, where T_i=b+sum_{k=i}^n q_k>0. (II)
For i<n this follows by combining the last two displayed inequalities; for i=n it is a_n>=p_n+q_n/2=b+q_n=T_n.

4. A finite-dimensional second-moment inequality.
Expand the nonnegative expectation of a squared norm:
0<=E||X-sum_{i=0}^n(a_i/q_i)g_i||^2
=1-2sum_{i=0}^n a_i^2/q_i+sum_{i=0}^n a_i^2/q_i
=1-sum_{i=0}^n a_i^2/q_i.
The cross terms in the double sum vanish by expected orthogonality. Since a_i>=T_i>0 and q_i>0, (II) gives
1>=sum_{i=0}^n T_i^2/q_i. (III)
This step works directly in the original finite-dimensional space and does not require that any random sample's gradients be orthogonal.

5. Eliminate the intermediate gradient energies exactly.
For 0<=k<=n, claim
sum_{i=0}^{k-1} T_i^2/q_i >= c_k T_k,
where the empty sum for k=0 is0. The claim holds at k=0. Suppose k<n and it holds at k. Since T_k=q_k+T_{k+1},
c_k T_k+T_k^2/q_k
=(c_k+1)q_k+(c_k+2)T_{k+1}+T_{k+1}^2/q_k.
The nonnegativity of
(sqrt(c_k+1)*sqrt(q_k)-T_{k+1}/sqrt(q_k))^2
bounds the sum of the first and third terms below by 2sqrt(c_k+1)T_{k+1}. Therefore the last display is at least c_{k+1}T_{k+1}, proving the induction.
Taking k=n in this claim and adding the final term gives from (III)
1>=c_n T_n+T_n^2/q_n
=q_n z(z+c_n),
where z=T_n/q_n=1+b/q_n>=1.
Since 2p_n=2b+q_n=q_n(2z-1), it follows that
2p_n <= (2z-1)/(z(z+c_n)).
Put r=(1+sqrt(1+2c_n))/2. Then c_n=2r(r-1) and r>=1. The exact identity
z(z+c_n)-r^2(2z-1)=(z-r)^2>=0
implies
(2z-1)/(z(z+c_n))<=1/r^2=beta_n.
Because ||x_0-x_star||^2=1 in each realization, 2p_n is the expected normalized error in the statement.

6. Estimate and asymptotics for beta_n.
An induction gives c_k>=k^2: it holds at0; if it holds at k, then
c_{k+1}=c_k+2+2sqrt(c_k+1)>=k^2+2+2k>=(k+1)^2.
The relation c_n=2r(r-1)<=2r^2 now gives beta_n=1/r^2<=2/c_n<=2/n^2 for n>=1.
For the asymptotic assertion put s_k=sqrt(c_k+1), so s_0=1 and
s_{k+1}=sqrt((s_k+1)^2+1).
Rationalization gives
s_{k+1}-s_k=1+1/(sqrt((s_k+1)^2+1)+s_k+1).
Thus s_k>=k+1 and
s_{k+1}-s_k<=1+1/[2(k+2)].
Summing shows
k+1<=s_k<=k+1+(1/2)sum_{j=2}^{k+1}1/j
<=k+1+(1/2)log(k+1),
where the last bound follows by integrating 1/t on intervals [j-1,j]. Consequently s_k/k tends to1 and c_k/k^2 tends to1. Substitution into beta_n gives n^2 beta_n tending to2.
Finally, 0<=expected normalized error/V_n<=beta_n/V_n, which tends to0 whenever n^2V_n tends to infinity. Every bound depends only on n, giving the asserted uniformity. No interchange of infimum and expectation has occurred.

## intuition
Independent or centered fresh random gradient directions force too much initial-distance energy through global interpolation. This target-specific impossibility result rules out expected-orthogonality adversaries before pursuing correlated nonlinear geometry; it is the worker's one selected initial-wave verification submission.
