---
fact_id: 1252b8823bb5a290
problem_id: gd-exact-profile-20260926
author: max2
predecessors: []
glossary_introduces:
  A_quad: Diagonal positive quadratic Hessian with distinct eigenvalues strictly between0 and1, used for strict feasibility.
  D_i: Elementary normalized trajectory bound product_(t<i)(1+h_t).
  E_red: All distinct ordered prefix pairs together with all outgoing terminal pairs (n,j), j in J_pre.
  G_pr: Full primal Gram matrix of the formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), or its fixed point in the separation argument.
  H_j: Conjugate lower quadratic cjet_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||²/2 for a prefix jet.
  J_pre: Prefix sample index set {star,0,...,n-1}, or {star} for n=0.
  M_cert: Full formal-vector quadratic certificate q_cert||x_0||²-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij after GD substitution.
  Mhat_cert: Full PSD certificate matrix for the positive compressed schedule.
  Q_ij: Signed normalized smooth-convex interpolation residual f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||²/2.
  R_alpha: The complete prefix quadratic remainder in M_cert=||g_n-V_alpha||²+R_alpha.
  T_pick: Linear coordinate-selection map from all original formal vectors to those used by the compressed certificate.
  V_alpha: x_n-sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j y_j, independent of the formal terminal gradient.
  alpha_j: Nonnegative terminal outgoing probability lambda_(n,j)/2 for j in J_pre.
  cjet_j: Conjugate jet value <g_j,x_j>-f_j in this terminal-extension result.
  delta_j: Prefix value f_j-||g_j||²/2, with delta_star=0.
  ghat_s: Compressed formal gradient chosen from the original positive-step indices, with ghat_m=g_n.
  hhat: Positive-step compression of the original nonnegative schedule.
  i_s: Original index of the s-th positive step in the zero-step compression argument.
  iota: Injection of compressed sample indices into the original indices, mapping the compressed terminal to original n.
  lambda^pre: Nonnegative interpolation multipliers on the complete queried prefix.
  lambda_ij: Nonnegative multiplier for the signed ordered interpolation residual Q_ij.
  lambdahat: Tight full certificate for the positive compressed schedule.
  q_cert: A real objective upper-certificate level; selected tight level is W_n(h).
  v(x): Unique vector maximizing <x,v>-H(v) in the explicitly constructed conjugate F.
  w_opt: The exact reduced/full objective value W_n(h) used in the separating-hyperplane argument.
  w_t: Cumulative terminal probability alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j, with w_(-1)=alpha_star.
  xhat_s: Compressed formal position x_0-sum_(r<s)h_(i_r)g_(i_r), equal to the mapped original position.
  y_j: Prefix proximal point x_j-g_j, with y_star=0.
external_refs: []
---

## statement
Exact unqueried-terminal reduction and full-certificate re-selection for arbitrary nonnegative gradient-descent schedules.

For every integer n>=0, every L>0, and every finite h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) in [0,infinity)^n, let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, with x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). The empty schedule n=0 is allowed.

For the following finite formulations use normalized smoothness1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0, normalized sampled values f_i, formal gradients g_i, and x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. Put I_n={star,0,...,n}, J_pre={star,0,...,n-1}, and Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 for distinct i,j in I_n. When n=0, J_pre={star}.

(a) The finite Gram maximization of 2f_n subject to G_pr>=0, G_pr_(x0,x0)<=1, all Q_ij<=0 with distinct i,j in J_pre, and all Q_(n,j)<=0 with j in J_pre, has attained optimal value W_n(h). Here G_pr is the full Gram matrix of the n+2 formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), with no rank constraint. No incoming terminal inequality Q_(j,n)<=0 is imposed. For any feasible prefix jets in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space, define cjet_j=<g_j,x_j>-f_j, H_j(v)=cjet_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||^2/2, H(v)=max_(j in J_pre)H_j(v), and F(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H(v)). Then F is an everywhere finite convex differentiable 1-smooth function interpolating all prefix jets, F(0)=0=min F, and it is the pointwise maximal such prefix interpolant. For every reduced-feasible terminal pair (g_n,f_n), F(x_n)>=f_n. At an optimal reduced transcript, F(x_n)=f_n and grad F(x_n)=g_n; hence every optimal reduced transcript is fully interpolable and obeys the deleted incoming terminal inequalities.

(b) There exist nonnegative multipliers lambda_ij on all distinct pairs in I_n, with lambda_(j,n)=0 for every j in J_pre, finite-node flow sum_j lambda_ij-sum_j lambda_ji=2*1_(i=n), and a FULL positive semidefinite quadratic certificate
M_cert=W_n(h)||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij
after the GD substitution. This certificate is PSD on the original formal space (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), including every gradient at a zero step, without imposing equality between duplicated formal gradients. Thus some tight full certificate has no incoming edge at the unqueried terminal sample for every nonnegative schedule. For schedules with positive steps this follows from an explicit strict primal and a separation argument. If there are zero steps, choose the original index of each positive step as the representative of its compressed query and the original index n as the terminal representative; embedding a certificate for the positive-step compression gives the asserted full-space certificate. The all-zero and empty schedules have W_n(h)=1 and the direct certificate lambda_(n,star)=2, all other lambda zero, with M_cert=||x_0-g_n||^2.

(c) For any real certificate level q_cert and any h as above, certificates with no incoming terminal multipliers are parameterized exactly as follows. Choose alpha_j>=0 for j in J_pre, with sum_j alpha_j=1, and nonnegative prefix multipliers lambda^pre_ij for distinct i,j in J_pre whose finite prefix flow is out_i^pre-in_i^pre=2alpha_i for 0<=i<n, where out_i^pre=sum_(j in J_pre,j!=i)lambda^pre_ij and in_i^pre=sum_(j in J_pre,j!=i)lambda^pre_ji. Define y_j=x_j-g_j, delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2, V_alpha=x_n-sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j y_j, with y_star=delta_star=0, and
R_alpha=q_cert||x_0||^2-2sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j delta_j-||V_alpha||^2+sum_(i!=j in J_pre)lambda^pre_ij Q_ij.
The prefix flow cancels all f_i in R_alpha. Set lambda_(n,j)=2alpha_j, all incoming terminal multipliers zero, and retain lambda^pre. The complete identity is
M_cert=||g_n-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha.
Consequently full PSD of M_cert is equivalent to PSD of the entire prefix quadratic R_alpha in (x_0,g_0,...,g_(n-1)). For n>=1, with w_(-1)=alpha_star and w_t=alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j, one has 0<=w_(-1)<=w_0<=...<=w_(n-1)=1 and
V_alpha=alpha_star x_0-sum_(t<n)[h_t w_t-(w_t-w_(t-1))]g_t.
For n=0, alpha_star=1, V_alpha=x_0, and R_alpha=(q_cert-1)||x_0||^2. Any actual normalized trajectory attaining a feasible q_cert has g_n=V_alpha, R_alpha=0, and multiplier complementarity.

This result deletes no interpolation inequalities inside the queried prefix. It asserts existence of a selected optimal certificate, not that every optimal certificate has the selected support. It neither establishes Huber tightness nor removes earlier remote forward edges, transfers earlier-cut stationarity under arbitrary re-selection, or proves the project's asymptotic exact-profile conjecture.

## proof
1. Normalization, interpolation necessity, and elementary bounds.

For an original instance let R=||x_0-x_star||>0. The normalized function
F_0(z)=[f(x_star+R z)-f(x_star)]/(L R^2)
is convex differentiable with gradient Lipschitz constant1, a minimizer0,
and initial norm1. Its GD uses the same h and its normalized objective
is 2F_0(z_n). This also proves the converse scaling correspondence.
We may work with smoothness1 and the optimum sample zero.

For a differentiable function K with 1-Lipschitz gradient, integration
of its gradient along a segment gives
K(x+d)<=K(x)+<grad K(x),d>+||d||^2/2.
For convex K, apply this to K(z)-<grad K(x_i),z>, whose minimum is
at x_i, taking a unit negative-gradient step from x_j. Its minimum
value is no larger than the stepped value, so
K(x_j)-K(x_i)-<grad K(x_i),x_j-x_i>
    >=||grad K(x_j)-grad K(x_i)||^2/2.
This is exactly Q_ij<=0 for the actual samples, with our stated
orientation of Q.

For a normalized legal instance, put D_i=product_(t<i)(1+h_t), D_0=1.
Since grad K(0)=0, ||g_i||<=||x_i|| and ||x_(i+1)||<=(1+h_i)||x_i||.
Thus ||x_i||,||g_i||<=D_i, and
0<=K(x_i)<=||x_i||^2/2.
In particular W_n(h)<=D_n^2<infinity.
It is strictly positive: choose the scalar quadratic K(x)=kappa*x^2/2
with kappa=1/[2(1+sum_t h_t)] in (0,1], starting at1.
Every factor 1-kappa h_t is positive, and its normalized error is
kappa*product_t(1-kappa h_t)^2>0.

2. Explicit interpolation and the maximal prefix extension.

Let any finite collection of prefix jets (x_j,g_j,f_j), j in J_pre,
satisfy every ordered prefix inequality and include the zero optimum jet.
Define cjet_j,H_j,H,F as in the statement, on the same finite-dimensional
Euclidean space. The function H(v)-||v||^2/2 is the maximum of finitely
many affine functions and hence is convex. H is continuous, finite,
1-strongly convex, and has quadratic growth. For each x, the function
<v,x>-H(v) therefore has a unique maximizer, denoted v(x).

The maximizer condition is x in partial H(v(x)). For any x,x',
the two strong subgradient inequalities for H, added together, give
<x-x',v(x)-v(x')>>=||v(x)-v(x')||^2.
Consequently ||v(x)-v(x')||<=||x-x'||.
The definition of F, evaluated at v(x) and v(x'), gives
0<=F(x')-F(x)-<v(x),x'-x>
    <=<v(x')-v(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.
Thus F is differentiable with grad F(x)=v(x), and its gradient is
1-Lipschitz. It is convex as a supremum of affine functions and finite
by the stated quadratic growth.

For i,j in J_pre direct expansion gives H_j(g_i)-a_i=Q_ij.
The term with j=i equals zero and every other term is nonpositive;
hence H(g_i)=a_i. The active quadratic H_i has gradient x_i at g_i,
and H>=H_i with equality there, so x_i is a subgradient of H at g_i.
The unique maximizer defining F(x_i) is consequently g_i, and
F(x_i)=<x_i,g_i>-a_i=f_i. This proves interpolation of all prefix jets.
In particular F(0)=0 and grad F(0)=0, so zero is a global minimizer.
The same construction, for any finite index set including the zero
jet, proves sufficiency of ALL pairwise Q inequalities for a legal
convex 1-smooth interpolant; repeated positions cause no exception.

If K is any other legal function interpolating the prefix, apply the
necessity calculation in paragraph1 between its sample at an arbitrary
x and each prefix jet j. Writing g=grad K(x), those inequalities give
H_j(g)<=<x,g>-K(x) for every j. Thus
K(x)<=<x,g>-H(g)<=F(x).
This proves the claimed pointwise maximality without importing an
interpolation or conjugacy theorem from a paper.

3. Exact value of the reduced Gram problem and primal attainment.

Realize any feasible Gram matrix by vectors in dimension at most n+2;
if its rank is zero, one may embed its zero vectors in R.
The complete prefix inequalities give the function F of paragraph2.
Its gradients at every queried prefix point equal the supplied g_i,
so the GD recursion reaches the same supplied x_n. No terminal gradient
is used to determine x_n, even when some steps vanish.

For the reduced terminal pair, the retained inequalities give
H_j(g_n)<=<x_n,g_n>-f_n for all j in J_pre. Therefore
f_n<=<x_n,g_n>-H(g_n)<=F(x_n).
If ||x_0||>0, normalization of this actual function yields
2F(x_n)<=W_n(h)||x_0||^2<=W_n(h).
If ||x_0||=0, all actual prefix iterates and x_n are the minimizer0,
so F(x_n)=0 and 2f_n<=0<W_n(h).
Thus the reduced supremum is at most W_n(h).
Every actual legal normalized trajectory is feasible for the reduced
Gram problem, so the reverse inequality holds.

For clarity the supremum is attained. Consider first the FULL Gram
problem, including every incoming terminal inequality. Its feasible
set is closed. Paragraph2 applied to the entire finite index set
I_n realizes every feasible point as a legal function; hence paragraph1
bounds all sampled gradients, all Gram entries by Cauchy--Schwarz,
and all values 0<=f_i<=D_i^2/2. The feasible set is therefore compact
in finite-dimensional matrix/value space. It is nonempty and has an
objective maximizer. Necessity and the interpolation construction
show its value is exactly W_n(h). The same full optimizer is reduced
feasible, so it also attains the reduced value.

At any reduced optimizer, the chain
W_n(h)=2f_n<=2F(x_n)<=W_n(h)||x_0||^2<=W_n(h)
has equal endpoints and W_n(h)>0. Thus ||x_0||=1,
f_n=F(x_n), and <x_n,g_n>-H(g_n)=F(x_n).
The maximizer of that conjugate expression is unique, so
g_n=grad F(x_n). The supplied terminal jet therefore belongs to the
same legal F as the prefix and satisfies every deleted incoming
inequality. This also proves the assertion for every reduced optimizer,
not just one optimizer chosen from the full feasible set.

4. Dual attainment for positive schedules, by separation.

Assume n>=1 and h_t>0 for every t<n. Let E_red be the retained
ordered pair set:
{(i,j): i,j in J_pre, i!=j} union {(n,j):j in J_pre}.
Use the matrix/value variables of the reduced primal.

There is a strictly feasible point with G_pr positive definite,
G_pr_(x0,x0)<1, and every retained Q strictly negative.
Choose n+2 distinct real theta_l in (0,1), let A_quad be the diagonal
matrix with these entries, let K(x)=<x,A_quad x>/2, and choose x_0
with every coordinate nonzero and norm less than1.
Write Poly_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), including Poly_0=1.
The columns (x_0,g_0,...,g_n) are evaluations at the theta_l of the
n+2 polynomials 1,z Poly_0,...,z Poly_n, times nonzero initial
coordinate factors. These polynomials have distinct degrees
0,1,...,n+1 and nonzero leading coefficients. A polynomial of degree
at most n+1 vanishing at n+2 distinct nodes is zero, so the evaluation
matrix is invertible and G_pr is positive definite.
For i!=j finite, Poly_i-Poly_j is a nonzero polynomial of degree
at most n; it cannot vanish at all chosen nodes. Poly_i cannot vanish
at all nodes either. Thus all finite sample positions are distinct
from each other and from star. Direct substitution for this quadratic
gives, with I the identity matrix,
Q_ij=-<x_i-x_j,A_quad(I-A_quad)(x_i-x_j)>/2<0.
This proves the full stated strictness.

Let w_opt=W_n(h), which is finite by paragraph1. Consider the open
convex set O of tuples (s,t,c), with s in R^(E_red) and t,c real,
for which there exist G_pr>=0 and real
sample values f with
s_ij>Q_ij(f,G_pr) for ij in E_red,
t>G_pr_(x0,x0)-1, and c<2f_n.
It is open since it is a union of open orthants; it is convex by
linearity of every Q in the Gram/value variables and convexity
of the PSD cone. The point (0,0,w_opt) is not in O.
Finite-dimensional separation of a point from a nonempty open convex
set gives a nonzero linear functional, chosen nonnegative on O
relative to that point. Independent upward changes of each s_ij
and of t, and downward changes of c, force its coefficients to be
(lambda_ij,rho,-eta) with lambda_ij,rho,eta>=0.
Taking limits to the boundary of each strict inequality yields,
for ALL G_pr>=0 and ALL real sample values f,
sum_(E_red)lambda_ij Q_ij(f,G_pr)
    +rho(G_pr_(x0,x0)-1)-eta*2f_n >= -eta*w_opt.       (S)

If eta=0, some lambda_ij or rho is positive, since the functional is
nonzero. Substitution of the strict primal point constructed above
makes the left side of (S) strictly negative, a contradiction.
Hence eta>0 and we divide the coefficients by eta to set eta=1.

The f_i are unrestricted real variables in (S). Their coefficients
must vanish:
out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n), for 0<=i<=n.
After their cancellation, (S) says
trace(M_cert(rho,lambda,h) G_pr)-rho>=-w_opt for every G_pr>=0.
If this matrix had a negative quadratic direction, scaling the
corresponding rank-one PSD G_pr would contradict this inequality.
It is therefore PSD. Taking G_pr=0 gives rho<=w_opt.
Conversely the flow, lambda>=0, and full PSD give, for every reduced
feasible point,
2f_n<=rho G_pr_(x0,x0)<=rho
because rho>=0. Thus w_opt<=rho. Equality follows.
Setting lambda_(j,n)=0 on the omitted pairs gives the required
tight full certificate on the original positive-step formal space.

5. Zero-step compression and lifting in the ORIGINAL formal space.

Suppose n>=1 and exactly m>=1 of the original steps are positive.
List their original indices as
0<=i_0<i_1<...<i_(m-1)<=n-1,
and define the positive compressed schedule hhat_s=h_(i_s),
0<=s<m. Deleting zero steps changes no actual iterate; therefore
W_m(hhat)=W_n(h) by the same function/initial-point trajectories in
both directions.

Apply paragraph4 to hhat, obtaining a tight incoming-terminal-free
certificate lambdahat on {star,0,...,m}.
Define the injective index map
iota(star)=star, iota(s)=i_s for 0<=s<m, and iota(m)=n.
On the original formal space define
xhat_0=x_0, ghat_s=g_(i_s) for s<m, ghat_m=g_n.
Its compressed formal positions satisfy
xhat_s=x_0-sum_(r<s)h_(i_r)g_(i_r)=x_(i_s) for s<m,
and
xhat_m=x_0-sum_(r<m)h_(i_r)g_(i_r)=x_n.
These are polynomial identities in independent original formal vectors:
the omitted coefficients h_t are ZERO. They do not use any equality
between gradients sampled at identical actual positions.

Put lambda_(iota(s),iota(t))=lambdahat_(s,t) on mapped pairs, and
put every other original multiplier equal to zero.
At each mapped finite node the original flow equals its compressed
flow; at each unmapped node both in-flow and out-flow vanish.
The mapped terminal is n, so the original finite flow is exactly
out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n). No multiplier points into original n.
Every mapped Q expression equals the corresponding compressed
Q expression after identifying the mapped values, whose coefficients
cancel by flow.

Let T_pick be the linear map selecting
(x_0,g_(i_0),...,g_(i_(m-1)),g_n)
from (x_0,g_0,...,g_n). The ORIGINAL full certificate matrix is precisely
T_pick^T Mhat_cert T_pick and is PSD because Mhat_cert is PSD.
Unmapped gradient columns are zero. No duplicate-gradient constraint
or auxiliary squared penalty is imposed or needed. This proves the
claimed original-index, full-formal-space lift, including leading,
interior, and trailing zero steps.

If m=0, including the empty schedule n=0, every actual iterate is x_0.
Smoothness at the minimizer gives 2f(x_0)<=||x_0||^2, and the scalar
quadratic x^2/2 attains equality. Thus W_n(h)=1.
Set lambda_(n,star)=2 and every other multiplier to zero.
It has exactly the required flow and no incoming terminal edges.
Since x_n=x_0 as a formal identity, its full quadratic is
||x_0||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,star)=||x_0-g_n||^2.
This treats all-zero schedules and n=0 without any Slater assertion
about duplicated points. Parts(a) and the maximal extension remain
valid independently for these schedules by paragraphs2--3.

6. Exact terminal-square and full prefix-PSD parameterization.

Take ANY certificate whose incoming terminal multipliers vanish.
The finite terminal flow gives sum_(j in J_pre)lambda_(n,j)=2.
Consequently alpha_j=lambda_(n,j)/2 is a nonnegative probability
vector. All other multipliers lie inside the prefix, and their finite
flow is out_i^pre-in_i^pre=2alpha_i for i<n.
For any such alpha and prefix multipliers define y_j,delta_j,V_alpha,
R_alpha as in the statement, taking the star quantities equal to zero.

Expanding the outgoing terminal sum gives
2sum_j alpha_j Q_(n,j)
 =2f_n-2sum_j alpha_j f_j
       +2<g_n,sum_j alpha_j x_j-x_n>
       +sum_j alpha_j||g_n-g_j||^2
 =2f_n-2sum_j alpha_j delta_j
       +||g_n-V_alpha||^2-||V_alpha||^2.
Substituting into the full certificate proves
M_cert=||g_n-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha.
In R_alpha the coefficient of each finite f_i is
(out_i^pre-in_i^pre)-2alpha_i=0; it contains no f_n.
It is the complete quadratic form on ALL original prefix vectors.
The vector V_alpha is independent of the formal terminal gradient g_n.
Hence full PSD is equivalent to prefix PSD: prefix PSD implies full
PSD from the square identity, and full PSD implies prefix PSD by
setting the free formal vector g_n equal to V_alpha.
This is true even with zero-step duplicates; it is not a test only
on legal transcripts.

Conversely any alpha and nonnegative prefix multipliers with the
stated prefix flow define a full multiplier family by
lambda_(n,j)=2alpha_j and incoming terminal multipliers zero.
Their full finite flow is the required objective flow. If R_alpha
is PSD the full square identity certifies it. This proves both
directions of the parameterization for any real q_cert.

For n>=1, expand positions in
V_alpha=x_n-sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j(x_j-g_j).
The coefficient of x_0 is
1-sum_(j=0)^(n-1)alpha_j=alpha_star.
For t<n, the coefficient of g_t is
-h_t+alpha_t+h_t sum_(j=t+1)^(n-1)alpha_j
 =-h_t[alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j]+alpha_t.
With w_t as in the statement, alpha_t=w_t-w_(t-1), giving
the claimed chronological formula and the nondecreasing w sequence.
For n=0 the probability simplex contains only alpha_star=1,
there are no prefix pairs, V_alpha=x_0, and
R_alpha=(q_cert-1)||x_0||^2.

Finally take an actual normalized trajectory attaining a feasible
certificate level q_cert. The certificate identity evaluated there is
M_cert=sum lambda_ij Q_ij<=0, while full PSD makes it nonnegative.
Every term lambda_ij Q_ij is nonpositive, so each is zero.
The square identity and prefix PSD then imply
g_n=V_alpha and R_alpha=0. This is an assertion about matching
actual trajectories, not a presumed rank condition on a primal.

7. Scope and proof dependencies.

The proof uses elementary smoothness, the displayed explicit convex
conjugate construction, finite-dimensional linear algebra, compactness
of the bounded finite Gram system, and a fully specified separating
hyperplane argument. No historical project KKT claim or unverified
finding is a premise. No external paper theorem is used as a black
box. In particular the separation argument is used only for genuinely
positive compressed steps, and the zero-step conclusion is obtained
by an explicit embedding into the original formal space.

Only incoming constraints at n, which is unqueried by the recursion,
are removed. Every ordered interpolation constraint inside J_pre
remains. The statement implies neither Huber tightness nor a removal
of earlier remote edges or a transfer of earlier-cut stationarity
under arbitrary re-selection; it does not decide the asymptotic
exact-profile problem.

## intuition
Only the final gradient is unqueried. The maximum of the prefix conjugate quadratics constructs a maximal legal extension at the already fixed final position, making all incoming terminal constraints value-redundant. Strict positive-step duality supplies a selected certificate, and choosing the query indices of positive steps embeds it into the original formal space when zero steps are present. The terminal outgoing multipliers sum to two, yielding an exact full Schur-square decomposition. No statement about removing queried-prefix edges or global Huber tightness is included.

  C1: Part B leaf capacity 1+A=2+a.
  C_D: Full polar-cone minimum set {z:<z,g><=0 for all g in D}.
  C_E: Polar minimum cone inside E, with ambient cone C_E+E^perp.
  C_L: Part B left-word capacity 1+s_L in the actual-auxiliary bridge identity.
  C_i: Part A value correction eta_i H_i+eta_i^2/2, distinct from schedule capacities in Part B.
  D0: Arbitrary nonempty compact convex set containing zero in Part B, fixed throughout its physical and auxiliary samples.
  D_R: Part A convex hull of zero and v_i=(g_i,eta_i/R_lift); subscript R denotes the lift scale.
  D_{R_lift,epsilon}: Part A simplex conv{0,w^1,...,w^m_A}, with w^b=(g^b,eta^b/R_lift,epsilon e_b).
  Delta1: Part B H2 slack C1^2-beta(2C1+beta).
  Delta2: Part B H3-root slack C^2-k(2C+k).

  F_D: Projection-gradient function max over g in the fixed compact convex D of <x,g>-||g||^2/2.
  H_i: In Part A only: cumulative height sum_(t<i)h_t eta_t. In Parts B/C H_j denotes the recursively defined schedule, never this scalar.
  K0: Part B complete polar minimum cone of D0; the same set as C_D0 in Part C.
  K1: Part B H2 multiplier (3C1+beta)/(C1-beta).
  K2: Part B H3-root multiplier (3C+k)/(C-k).
  LR: Part B actual right-child radial term Lrad(s,y,d).
  Lambda_ij: Part B eight nonnegative H2 row multipliers explicitly listed in (3.6), all others zero.
  Lrad: Part B exact radial term 2t<z,P(z)-P(z/d)>+t^2(||P(z)||^2-||P(z/d)||^2), nonnegative for d>=1.
  N_ib: Part A unperturbed projection normal <z_i-v^(a(i)),v^b-v^(a(i))>.
  Np: Part B actual nonnegative normal <v-a_p,a_p-r>.
  Nr: Part B actual nonnegative normal <w-D r,r-a_p>.
  P_D: Euclidean projection onto the specified compact convex set D.
  P_E: Orthogonal projection onto E=span{w^1,...,w^m_A} in Part A.
  P_L: Part B local capacity 1+sum(left word).
  P_R: Part B local capacity 1+sum(right word).
  Pi_i: Part A finite stability product product_(t<i)(1+h_t).
  RP: Part B actual left-prefix remainder Rem(x,M,zP).
  RR: Part B actual right-child remainder Rem(w,D,zR).
  R_lift: Part A positive finite lift scale, called R in the frozen source; unrelated to the real number space R and to normalization R_n.
  R_prim: Part B off-root coefficient (P_L+t)[P_L P_R-t(P_L+P_R+t)]/(P_R-t).
  R_prim1: Part B H2 specialization (C1+beta)Delta1/(C1-beta).
  R_prim2: Part B H3-root specialization (C+k)Delta2/(C-k).
  Rem: Part B exact nonnegative maximization remainder M||p-bbar||^2+2<v-Mp,p-bbar>, p=P(v/M).
  S3: Sum of the seven entries of the same-enclosing-m H_3, also written S_3.
  T_H3: Actual seven-update composition for H_3 with gradient P_D0.
  Theta: Part B coefficient h[C_L D-k(C_L+D+k)]/(D+k), with h=C_L+k.
  U_d: For the fixed body D0, max over g in D0 of 2<x,g>-d||g||^2, for positive scalar d.
  V_b: Part B primitive energy of actual word b, given explicitly in Section 2; on a projection function equals ||input||^2-||endpoint+mass*P(endpoint)||^2.
  W_n^simplex: Restricted supremum in assertion (A), over full-dimensional origin-vertex simplices in dimensions 1 through p+1 with all legal starts and selected minimizers.
  Z_i: Part A perturbed actual iterate (x_i,-R_lift-H_i/R_lift,-epsilon b_i).
  a(i): Part A label of the gradient g_i among zero and the distinct nonzero gradients; every physical occurrence is retained.
  a_R: Part A nearby minimum (0,-R_lift).
  a_p: Part B actual bridge-query gradient P(v).
  b_i: Part A chronological vector sum_(t<i)h_t e_(a(t)); separately in Part B a word entry, as locally specified.
  beta_i: Part A value correction <b_i,e_(a(i))>+||e_(a(i))||^2/2.
  deltaA: Part B scalar 2-A^2 for the physical leaf step A=1+a.
  e_c: Part A terminal value error bound (1-c)f_n+c||g_n||B_n+c B_n^2/2.
  eta_i: Part A height <x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2; eta_star=0.
  m_A: Part A number of distinct nonzero sampled gradients, at most p+1; unrelated to Part B enclosing m.
  zP: Part B convex mixture [x-w+(D+h)r]/M for the actual left-prefix maximization remainder.
  zR: Part B convex mixture [w-y+(d+s)q]/D for the actual right-child maximization remainder.

---

## statement
Projection-simplex universality for finite words and exact full-class value of the actual seven-step H_3 block.

All spaces, norms, and inner products are finite-dimensional real Euclidean ones. For every integer n>=0, every finite predetermined nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), and every advertised L>0, define W_n(h) as the supremum of
    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)
over every convex differentiable function f with gradient Lipschitz constant at most L and an attained minimum, every selected minimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star, using the actual updates
    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i), 0<=i<n.
The schedule is fixed before the function and start are chosen. Empty words and zero steps are included. Write R_n(h)=W_n(h)/2, and p=#{i:0<=i<n and h_i>0}.

(A) For each fixed word h, W_n(h) equals the supremum restricted, at normalized smoothness one, to the functions
    F_D(x)=max_(g in D)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
    D=conv{0,w_1,...,w_k} in R^k, 1<=k<=p+1,
where w_1,...,w_k are linearly independent. Thus D is a full-dimensional simplex with the origin as a vertex. The restriction retains all starts, all selected minimizers unequal to the start, and the same actual word. Here conv denotes convex hull, P_D is Euclidean projection onto D, grad F_D=P_D, min F_D=0, and the complete minimum set is the polar cone
    C_D={z in R^k:<z,g><=0 for every g in D}.
For positive scores one may equivalently use the closest-minimum denominator dist(x_0,C_D)^2. For general L the restricted objectives are L F_D and the steps are h_i/L, preserving the same score. This is equality of suprema, with no assertion of finite-parameter exact representation or attainment for each source run, or uniform conditioning or approximation over words.

(B) For every integer m>=1536 set
    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<3.
Every child uses this same m and these same parameters. Let S3 be the sum of the seven entries of H_3. For every nonempty compact convex set D0 containing zero in any positive finite dimension, define, for d>0,
    U_d(x)=max_(g in D0)(2<x,g>-d||g||^2).
Let T_H3 be the actual composition of x -> x-h_i P_D0(x) over the seven original steps of H_3 in order. Then for every start x and every real d>=1,
    U_d(T_H3 x)<=U_(d+2S3)(x).
All physical and auxiliary projections use the same set D0. No interior or full-dimensionality assumption on D0 is required.

(C) For every m>=1536 and its actual seven-step H_3 from (B), the full original function class satisfies
    W_7(H_3)=1/(1+2S3).
The lower equality is attained by a one-dimensional Huber function with the original selected-minimum radius. These assertions make no all-depth H_j U claim and do not resolve the original asymptotic minimax problem.

## proof
The sole declared predecessor is 1252b8823bb5a290, used only for its
smooth-convex interpolation necessity; Part A also derives the scaled
inequality directly. Every new claim in (A)-(C) is proved below, and no
unverified report or audit is a mathematical premise. Symbols and equation
numbers have the local scopes explicitly specified in each proof part.

## Proof Part A: finite-word projection-simplex universality

Fix the finite word h from (A). In this part S=sum_(i<n)h_i, p is its number of positive entries, and W_n^simplex(h) denotes the restricted supremum in (A). The local number of distinct nonzero gradients is m_A; it is unrelated to the enclosing schedule parameter m of Parts B and C. The local lift scale is R_lift. All numbered references in a part refer to that same part unless another part is explicitly named.

## 2. Elementary projection facts and the complete minimum set

For a nonempty compact convex set `D`, the maximizer defining `F_D` is unique:
completing the square identifies it with the unique nearest point `P_D(q)`.
For `v in D`, the exact characterization is

    P_D(q)=v  iff  <q-v,w-v><=0 for every w in D.     (2.1)

Necessity follows by differentiating the squared distance on the segment
from `v` to `w` at zero. For sufficiency, expand

    ||q-w||^2 = ||q-v||^2 + ||w-v||^2
                -2<q-v,w-v> >= ||q-v||^2.

For a finite convex hull it is enough to check every generating point,
because the left side of (2.1) is affine in `w`.

The two normal inequalities for `v=P_D(q)` and `v'=P_D(q')` imply

    <q-q',v-v'> >= ||v-v'||^2.

Thus the projection is 1-Lipschitz. Evaluating the affine maxima at each
other's optimizers gives

    0 <= F_D(q')-F_D(q)-<P_D(q),q'-q>
      <= <P_D(q')-P_D(q),q'-q> <= ||q'-q||^2.

Consequently `F_D` is differentiable with gradient `P_D` and is globally
1-smooth. It is convex as a maximum of affine functions of `q` and finite
because `D` is compact.

When `0 in D`, the minimum is zero and its COMPLETE minimum set is the cone

    C_D = { a : <a,v><=0 for every v in D }.          (2.2)

Membership makes every defining expression nonpositive, while `v=0` gives
zero. Conversely, if `<a,v>>0` for some `v in D`, then `theta v in D` for
`0<=theta<=1`, and

    <a,theta v>-||theta v||^2/2 > 0

for sufficiently small positive `theta`. Thus `a` is not a minimizer.
The polar here always means the cone with threshold ZERO, not a body polar
with threshold one. It is nonempty, closed, and convex, so every point has
a closest point in it. Existence follows, for example, by restricting a
distance-minimizing sequence to a bounded ball and taking a convergent
subsequence.

## 3. Strict smoothness supplies the necessary strict rows

Let `0<ell<1`, let `f` be convex differentiable and ell-smooth, and translate
and subtract its chosen optimum so that `f(0)=min f=0`. Use its ACTUAL GD
samples `i=0,...,n`, including the terminal sample, with

    x_{i+1}=x_i-h_i g_i,
    g_i=grad f(x_i),  f_i=f(x_i).

Add a reference label `*` with `x_*=g_*=f_*=0`. Define

    Q_ji = f_j-f_i+<g_j,x_i-x_j>+||g_j-g_i||^2/2,
    gamma = (1-ell)/(2ell)>0.

The ell-smooth interpolation necessity is

    Q_ji <= -gamma ||g_j-g_i||^2.                   (3.1)

This is the smoothness-scaled necessity part of accepted local fact
`1252b8823bb5a290`; none of its duality or attainment conclusions is used.
One can also derive the precise orientation directly: the convex function
`k(x)=f(x)-<g_j,x>` has a minimum at `x_j`, and an ell-smooth descent step
from `x_i` of length `1/ell` gives

    k(x_j) <= k(x_i-(g_i-g_j)/ell)
             <= k(x_i)-||g_i-g_j||^2/(2ell).

Rearrange and add the `||g_i-g_j||^2/2` term to obtain (3.1).

Put

    eta_i = <x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
    H_i = sum_{t<i} h_t eta_t,
    eta_* = 0.

The optimum row `Q_{i,*}=-eta_i` gives

    eta_i >= gamma ||g_i||^2 >= 0,                 (3.2)

and `Q_{*,i}=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2` gives

    f_i-||g_i||^2/2 >= gamma ||g_i||^2.             (3.3)

In particular every nonzero gradient has STRICTLY positive `eta_i`.
If `g_i=0`, convexity makes `x_i` a minimizer, so `f_i=eta_i=0`.
All later gradients and positions then remain zero-gradient/stationary.

If `g_i=g_j=g`, the two elementary convexity inequalities force

    f_i-f_j=<g,x_i-x_j>.

Therefore `eta_i=eta_j`, and direct substitution gives `Q_ji=0`.
The same conclusion applies to equal zero gradients and to the reference
label. Distinct positions with the same gradient need not be identified as
positions; only their gradient/height vertex labels will be identified.

## 4. exact sparse normal-guard construction

For `R_lift>0`, define

    v_i=(g_i,eta_i/R_lift),  v_*=0,
    D_R=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n},
    z_i=(x_i,-R_lift-H_i/R_lift),
    a_R=(0,-R_lift).

This is a compact finite polytope in one extra coordinate, with at most
`n+2` generating points. The point `a_R` belongs to its full minimum cone
because `<a_R,v_i>=-eta_i<=0`. For `z_0=(x_0,-R_lift)`,

    ||z_0-a_R||=||x_0||.                           (4.1)

The claimed identity is exact. For every physical sample `i` and every
`j in {*,0,...,n}`,

    <z_i-v_i,v_j-v_i>
      = <x_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>-(eta_j-eta_i)
        -(H_i+eta_i)(eta_j-eta_i)/R_lift^2
      = Q_ji-(H_i+eta_i)(eta_j-eta_i)/R_lift^2.          (4.2)

For clarity, the middle terms expand to

    <x_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>-(eta_j-eta_i)
      = f_j-f_i+<g_j,x_i-x_j>+||g_j-g_i||^2/2.

This checks both the orientation `Q_ji` and the sign of the vertical
correction. No position associated with the reference `*` appears in the
normal test other than through the zero jet in `Q_*i`.

If `g_i=g_j`, both terms in (4.2) vanish. Otherwise `-Q_ji>0` by (3.1).
Define the data-dependent finite number

    A = max_{i=0,...,n; j in {*,0,...,n}; g_i!=g_j}
          (H_i+eta_i)(eta_i-eta_j)_+ / (-Q_ji),      (4.3)

with maximum over an empty set equal to zero. Finiteness follows from
finitely many finite data and strictly positive denominators. Here
`H_i+eta_i>=0` uses the nonnegative steps.

For `R_lift^2>=A`, all expressions in (4.2) are nonpositive. By (2.1),

    P_{D_R}(z_i)=v_i,  for EVERY physical i=0,...,n. (4.4)

The prospective positions satisfy

    z_{i+1}=z_i-h_i v_i,                            (4.5)

because `H_{i+1}=H_i+h_i eta_i`. Equations (4.4)--(4.5), by induction from
the stated initial point, prove that these are the ACTUAL ordinary GD
iterates on the single fixed function `F_{D_R}`. All original horizontal
positions are reproduced exactly. The added coordinate moves by
`-h_i eta_i/R_lift`; it is not held fixed.

The value at every actual sample is also exact:

    F_{D_R}(z_i)
      = <z_i,v_i>-||v_i||^2/2
      = f_i-C_i/R_lift^2,
    C_i = eta_i H_i+eta_i^2/2 >= 0.                (4.6)

The optimum/reference row is included in the guard; it is not left to a
heuristic value estimate. If `g_i!=0`, (4.4) already implies positive value
via `F_{D_R}(z_i)>=||v_i||^2/2>0`. Taking `R_lift` larger also makes the explicit
correction in (4.6) as small as desired.

This proves the sparse normal-guard construction. If only `R_lift^2=A` is used, some
distinct-vertex rows may be equalities; that boundary is insufficient for
the generic perturbation proposal. The next section deliberately chooses
strict quantitative slack instead.

## 5. Quantitative strict margins, including zero-gradient samples

Suppose at least one sampled gradient is nonzero. List the distinct nonzero
gradient values as `g^1,...,g^{m_A}`, with their well-defined heights
`eta^1,...,eta^{m_A}`. Write

    g^0=0,  eta^0=0,
    a(i) in {0,...,m_A},
    g_i=g^{a(i)},  eta_i=eta^{a(i)}.

The equality of heights proved above makes this label map unambiguous,
including repeated gradients at different physical times. Let

    delta = min_{0<=a<b<=m_A} ||g^a-g^b|| > 0.        (5.1)

Choose

    R_lift^2 >= max(1,2A).                              (5.2)

For a physical `i` and a target label `b`, define

    N_{ib}=<z_i-v^{a(i)},v^b-v^{a(i)}>,
    v^b=(g^b,eta^b/R_lift).

When `a(i)!=b`, choose any original representative of `b` (or the reference
label for zero) in (4.2). The possible positive correction is at most
`A(-Q_ji)/R_lift^2<=(-Q_ji)/2`. Thus

    N_{ib} <= Q_ji/2
            <= -(gamma/2)||g_i-g^b||^2
            <= -gamma delta^2/2.                  (5.3)

For equal labels it is identically zero. This retains every PHYSICAL
sample row, even when one vertex label occurs repeatedly; those samples
have different `H_i` and must not be silently collapsed as time indices.

If `g_i=0` and `b!=0`, the exact formula is even more transparent:

    N_{ib}=Q_{ji}-H_i eta^b/R_lift^2<0.

So zero-gradient samples supply strict inequalities against every nonzero
vertex. Their equality against zero itself is a harmless identical-vertex
row, not a missing strictness condition.

The strict margins also show that each distinct nonzero `v^b` is an exposed
vertex of `D_R`: at an occurrence of label `b`, the vector `z_i-v^b`
strictly exposes it among all other generating points. Zero is exposed by
`a_R`, since all `eta^b>0`. Thus, under (5.2), the sparse body already has
exactly `m_A+1` vertices, although they need not be affinely independent.

## 6. an explicit simplex perturbation with exact dynamics

Append `m_A` new mutually orthogonal coordinates. Let `e_1,...,e_(m_A)` be their
standard basis and set `e_0=0`. For a number `epsilon>0`, define

    w^b=(v^b,epsilon e_b),  0<=b<=m_A,
    D_{R_lift,epsilon}=conv{0,w^1,...,w^{m_A}}.

In particular `w^0=0`; a zero-gradient vertex is NOT given a new nonzero
component. Every occurrence of the same nonzero gradient receives exactly
the same component. Define cumulative chronological vectors

    b_i=sum_{t<i} h_t e_{a(t)},
    Z_i=(z_i,-epsilon b_i),
    A_R=(a_R,0).

The INITIAL appended coordinates are zero. Their subsequent values above
are recomputed from all the actual preceding steps, rather than assumed to
stay zero. By construction,

    Z_0=(x_0,-R_lift,0),
    Z_{i+1}=Z_i-h_i w^{a(i)}.                       (6.1)

The normal condition has an explicit perturbation identity. With
`a=a(i)`, for every target label `b`,

    <Z_i-w^a,w^b-w^a>
       = N_{ib}
         -epsilon^2 <b_i+e_a,e_b-e_a>.             (6.2)

There is no term linear in `epsilon`, because old and new coordinate blocks
are orthogonal and the new initial block is zero.

Let `b_{i,a}=<b_i,e_a>` for `a>0`, and take `b_{i,0}=0`. All these numbers
are nonnegative and at most `S`. The coefficient of `epsilon^2` in (6.2)
is exactly

    0                         if a=b;
    b_{i,a}-b_{i,b}+1          if a,b>0 and a!=b;
    b_{i,a}+1                  if a>0 and b=0;
    -b_{i,b}                   if a=0 and b>0.

Hence it is at most `S+1`. Equal-label rows remain exactly zero for every
physical occurrence. At zero-gradient samples the perturbation only makes
the distinct-vertex normal inequality more negative.

Choose, in addition to (5.2),

    0<epsilon^2 <= gamma delta^2/[4(S+1)].          (6.3)

Combining (5.3) and (6.2) gives, for all different labels,

    <Z_i-w^{a(i)},w^b-w^{a(i)}>
         <= -gamma delta^2/4 < 0.                 (6.4)

The identical-label rows are zero. Thus every generator satisfies the normal
condition, and (2.1) proves

    P_{D_{R_lift,epsilon}}(Z_i)=w^{a(i)}

at every physical index, including the terminal. Along with (6.1), this
proves that `Z_i` is the ACTUAL GD run of `F_{D_{R_lift,epsilon}}` under the same
word. This is a quantitative proof of the proposal, not an appeal to
genericity or continuity at a supposedly strict equality.

The vectors `w^1,...,w^{m_A}` are linearly independent: projection of a linear
relation to the new coordinate block gives `epsilon` times every coefficient
equal to zero. Therefore this body is a genuine `m_A`-simplex in its span,
with exactly `m_A+1` vertices including zero.

The nearby minimum remains exact:

    <A_R,w^b>=-eta^b<0 for b>0,
    ||Z_0-A_R||=||x_0||.                           (6.5)

Thus the whole minimum cone has not been replaced by the origin, and no
large artificial coordinate is paid for in the initial distance.

Finally, every perturbed value has the explicit formula

    F_{D_{R_lift,epsilon}}(Z_i)
      = f_i-C_i/R_lift^2-epsilon^2 beta_i,
    beta_i=<b_i,e_{a(i)}>+||e_{a(i)}||^2/2.         (6.6)

If `a(i)=0`, both `C_i` and `beta_i` are zero and the value is exactly zero.
Otherwise

    beta_i=b_{i,a(i)}+1/2,
    0<beta_i<=S+1/2.

This proves value continuity quantitatively while preserving the original
strict-run horizontal trajectory exactly before compression.

For any desired strict-run terminal value loss `tau>0`, the simultaneous
explicit choices

    R_lift^2 >= max(1,2A,2C_n/tau),
    0<epsilon^2 <= min{
        gamma delta^2/[4(S+1)],
        tau/[2(S+1/2)]
    }                                              (6.7)

make the total loss in (6.6) at most `tau`, while all different-label normal
guards remain strictly negative. Every bound is finite and strictly
positive where a positive perturbation is needed. No uniform lower bound
on `delta`, or uniform upper bound on `A`, is assumed.

## 7. Compression to exactly m_A dimensions and the own closest radius

The construction so far lives in the original space plus `1+m_A` coordinates.
Let

    E=span{w^1,...,w^{m_A}},  dim E=m_A,

and let `P_E` be orthogonal projection onto `E`. Since the entire body lies
in `E`, for every `Z` and every `w in D_{R_lift,epsilon}`,

    <Z,w>=<P_E Z,w>,
    ||Z-w||^2=||P_E Z-w||^2+||Z-P_E Z||^2.

Consequently both the value and the selected projection gradient are
preserved:

    F_D(Z)=F_D(P_E Z),
    P_D(Z)=P_D(P_E Z).

Put `q_i=P_E Z_i` and `a=P_E A_R`. Then

    q_{i+1}=q_i-h_i w^{a(i)},
    P_D(q_i)=w^{a(i)},
    F_D(q_i)=F_D(Z_i),
    <a,w^b>=-eta^b<=0.

Thus the compressed points are an actual GD run of the same simplex
function regarded as a function on the `m_A`-dimensional Euclidean space
`E`; choosing an orthonormal basis identifies it with `R^(m_A)`. The point `a`
is a true minimizer, and

    ||q_0-a||=||P_E(Z_0-A_R)||<=||x_0||.            (7.1)

This handles the selected radius. The OWN closest radius is preserved
exactly under the compression as well. Writing `C_E` for the polar cone
inside `E`, the ambient minimum set is

    C_D = C_E + E^perp.

Orthogonal decomposition therefore gives

    dist(Z_0,C_D)=dist(P_E Z_0,C_E).                 (7.2)

If the terminal value is positive, neither starting point is a minimizer:
a zero gradient would leave every following iterate fixed with zero value.
The shared radius in (7.2), denoted `rho`, is then positive, and (7.1) gives

    0<rho<=||x_0||.

For `r=||x_0||>0`, if `tau<f_n`, (6.7) consequently yields

    2 F_D(q_n)/rho^2 >= 2(f_n-tau)/r^2.             (7.3)

The positivity of the lower numerator is explicit; no inequality with a
negative numerator is reversed by changing the denominator. Neither the
selected compressed radius nor the closest radius is asserted to equal the
original `r`. The inequality is sufficient.

The number of distinct sampled positions is at most `p+1`, because a zero
step leaves the position unchanged and each positive step can add at most
one position. Therefore

    m_A <= p+1,
    number of simplex vertices = m_A+1 <= p+2.

This count includes the terminal gradient. Repeated gradients can reduce
`m_A` further, even when their positions differ. It is not legitimate to
remove a distinct terminal gradient merely because it is unqueried by the
last update; it remains part of this value/normal construction.

## 8. Regularization of an arbitrary 1-smooth original run

Return now to an arbitrary convex differentiable 1-smooth `f`, with chosen
minimum zero at zero and initial radius `r>0`. Its original samples are
`x_i,g_i,f_i`. For `0<c<1`, define `f_c=c f` and run the SAME word on it,
starting from the same `x_0`. Denote the new actual positions by `x_i^c`.
Its smoothness bound is `ell=c<1`, and its minimum set is unchanged.

Set

    M=max_{0<=i<=n}||g_i||,
    Pi_i=product_{t<i}(1+h_t),
    B_i=(1-c)M(Pi_i-1).

Subtract the two actual updates and use 1-Lipschitz continuity of `grad f`:

    ||x_{i+1}^c-x_{i+1}||
      <= (1+c h_i)||x_i^c-x_i||+(1-c)h_i||g_i||
      <= (1+h_i)||x_i^c-x_i||+(1-c)h_i M.

Induction gives

    ||x_i^c-x_i||<=B_i.                             (8.1)

Convexity and smoothness at the ORIGINAL terminal point imply

    |f(x_n^c)-f_n|<=||g_n||B_n+B_n^2/2.

Therefore the terminal error after scaling the objective is explicitly

    |f_c(x_n^c)-f_n| <= e_c,
    e_c=(1-c)f_n+c||g_n||B_n+c B_n^2/2 -> 0.        (8.2)

For example, with `K=f_n+M^2(Pi_n-1)+M^2(Pi_n-1)^2/2`, one has
`e_c<=(1-c)K`. For a positive original terminal value, `K>0`, so a finite
choice of `c<1` sufficiently close to one is immediate from this bound.

The exact horizontal reproduction in Sections 4--6 is of the ACTUAL
`f_c` run. There is no assertion that this regularized run is horizontally
identical to the original `f` run.

Given any positive original score and `eta>0`, choose

    0<d<min(f_n,eta r^2/2).

First choose `c<1` so that `e_c<d/2`; the new strict-run terminal value is
positive. Then choose a strict-run loss `0<tau<d/2`, and choose `R_lift,epsilon`
by (6.7) for THAT strict run. Its total value loss relative to the original
is less than `d`, so the resulting simplex run has positive terminal value
and own-closest score

    2 F_D(q_n)/rho^2
      >= 2(f_n-d)/r^2
      > 2f_n/r^2-eta.                              (8.3)

This sequence of choices is important: `c` first, then its finite actual
transcript, then `R_lift`, then a positive `epsilon`, then span compression.
The parameters may depend on the instance, the entire fixed word, and the
tolerance. The number of distinct gradient labels may also depend on `c`,
but it always satisfies the same bound `m_A<=p+1`.

## 9. Equality of suprema, scale L, and boundary audit

Every simplex function in assertion (A) is a legal convex differentiable 1-smooth
function with a minimizer, by Section 2. Thus

    W_n^simplex(h)<=W_n(h).

For every original positive-score instance and every tolerance, (8.3)
produces an admissible simplex instance with no more than `p+2` vertices
and dimension no more than `p+1`. Taking suprema gives the reverse
inequality and proves assertion (A).

Original zero-score runs do not require a ratio with zero closest radius.
The restricted class is nonempty and has nonnegative scores. It even has
a positive score for every fixed word: in dimension one take `D=[0,1]`
and start at `q_0=1+S`. Every iterate stays at least one, all projection
gradients equal one, and the terminal value is `1/2`, with closest radius
`1+S`. Its score is `1/(1+S)^2>0`.

For arbitrary `L>0`, replace the original function by

    tilde f(x)=[f(x_*+x)-f(x_*)]/L.

The normalized step is `h_i`, exactly corresponding to the original
`h_i/L` step. Multiply the final simplex function by `L`. Its gradient
is `L P_D`, its algorithm again uses the same word, and its normalized
score is still `2F_D(q_n)/rho^2`.

The complete boundary audit is as follows.

1. **Empty word and all-zero words.** Here `p=0`, positions do not move,
   there is at most one nonzero gradient label, and `H_i=b_i=0`. A positive
   score uses a one-dimensional segment simplex. Equations (4.6) and (6.6)
   reduce respectively to losses `eta_i^2/(2R_lift^2)` and `epsilon^2/2`.
   The regularization error (8.2) still applies with `Pi_n=1`.
2. **Zero steps among positive steps.** Duplicate positions and gradients
   use the same label. Both cumulative vectors simply omit their zero
   contributions, and no strictness between identical vertices is required.
3. **Repeated nonzero gradients at different times.** Convexity forces
   equal heights. Use one vertex and one appended coordinate for the whole
   label, but retain every physical normal row and its actual `H_i,b_i`.
   Equation (6.2) is identically zero for a same-label comparison regardless
   of accumulated time. Different-label comparisons have the explicit bound.
4. **Zero-gradient samples.** Their function values and heights are zero,
   and their vertex remains exactly zero. Against nonzero vertices their
   old rows are strict and their new correction is nonpositive. From such
   a sample onward the original and lifted runs are stationary. If the
   terminal score is positive, no zero-gradient sample can have occurred.
5. **All gradients zero.** The run has score zero. The exact sparse lift
   is the point body `{0}`. A zero-dimensional simplex convention can
   describe it, but no own-closest quotient is formed and this case is
   unnecessary for the positive-score supremum argument.
6. **Origin/optimum rows.** The reference zero vertex is included in `A`
   and in every perturbed normal test. Its perturbation coefficient at a
   nonzero sample is `b_{i,a}+1`, the potentially positive term controlled
   by `S+1`. It is not dropped. The chosen nearby minimizer is checked
   separately by its negative inner products with all nonzero vertices.
7. **Degenerate sparse bodies.** Degeneracy before the orthogonal
   perturbation causes no issue. Positive `epsilon` makes all nonzero
   vertices independent. Compression then gives precisely their span,
   without a full-dimensionality assumption on the original gradient data.
8. **Huge finite steps and small strict margins.** `A`, `S`, and `Pi_n` may
   be huge, while `gamma` and `delta` may be tiny. For a fixed finite run
   they remain finite, with `gamma,delta>0`, so (6.7) permits a finite `R_lift`
   and a strictly positive `epsilon`. No word-uniform conditioning estimate
   is asserted, and no limit is interchanged with a supremum or infimum.
9. **Closest-minimum distance.** Positivity is obtained before dividing;
   compression preserves the own closest radius exactly by (7.2), while
   the explicitly chosen minimum gives its upper bound by the original
   radius. No claim of convergence of these radii is needed.

The scope remains nonnegative finite steps and finite horizons. Negative
steps are not covered by the signs used in `H_i`, `b_i`, and the estimates.



## Proof Part B: the same-m actual H3 U identity

## 1. Fixed parameters, bodies, and notation

Fix ONE integer m>=1536, throughout all children. Define

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, C_j=1+S_j.

The physical H2 is (A,B,A), where A=1+a and B=1+beta. Write

    s=S2=2A+B, C=1+s, k=a*c^2=beta*c,
    h=C+k, S=S3=s+(1+k)+s=2C+k-1.

Thus h is the MASS of the prefix H2 followed by its root bridge, not an
extra physical step. The seven physical steps are
(A,B,A,1+k,A,B,A). Neither m nor the leaf parameters reset.

For one fixed nonempty compact convex set D0 in a finite-dimensional real
Euclidean space, containing0, put

    P(v)=argmin_(g in D0)||v-g||^2,
    F(v)=max_(g in D0)(<v,g>-||g||^2/2),
    U_d(v)=max_(g in D0)(2<v,g>-d||g||^2), d>0.

A physical word uses x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i and g_i=P(x_i).
All auxiliary projections in this proof also use this SAME D0.
No approximation to the physical trajectory is used.

The accepted interpolation orientation is fact1252b8823bb5a290:

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.       (1.1)

Only the interpolation necessity of predecessor 1252b8823bb5a290 is
used. All composition and H2 identities needed here are proved in this
part. For clarity, the scalar s here is S2; the constant sqrt(2)-1 is
denoted u. The primitive coefficient R_prim below is unrelated to the
lift scale R_lift of Part A or the objective normalization R_n.

## 2. Elementary projection facts and exact original rows

Projection optimality is

    <v-P(v),g-P(v)> <=0 for all g in D0.                 (2.1)

Necessity follows by minimizing squared distance on each segment from
P(v) to g. Sufficiency follows by expanding ||v-g||^2. The minimizer is
unique by strict convexity of squared distance. Adding the two conditions
for inputs v,w gives ||P(v)-P(w)||^2<=<v-w,P(v)-P(w)>; hence P is
1-Lipschitz. Completion of the square identifies P(v) as the unique
maximizer in F. The two maximizing inequalities bound the first-order
remainder of F by ||t||||P(v+t)-P(v)||, proving grad F=P. Thus F is
convex, differentiable and1-smooth.

Its minimum is0. The minimizers are exactly

    K0={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in D0}.

If z is in K0, all candidates in F(z) are nonpositive and g=0 attains0.
If some <z,g> is positive, sufficiently small positive multiples of g
belong to D0 and give a positive value. For every z_star in K0 and M>0,

    U_M(x)<=||x-z_star||^2/M,                           (2.2)

by completing the square in2<x-z_star,g>-M||g||^2 and using
<z_star,g><=0. No origin-radius replacement is made.

At physical or auxiliary samples of this same F, direct substitution of
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 into (1.1) gives

    Q_ij=<x_j-g_j,g_i-g_j>.                            (2.3)

Every row below is therefore an actual same-body projection normal.
Auxiliary samples introduce no physical gradient-descent step.

For a word b of mass t, input v, endpoint z, define its primitive energy

    V_b=2sum_(i<N)b_i(f_i-f_N)
        +sum_(i<N)b_i(1-b_i)||g_i||^2-t(t+1)||g_N||^2.

Telescoping ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2 proves

    V_b=||v||^2-||z+tP(z)||^2.                         (2.4)

Indeed the value and nonterminal gradient terms sum to
||v||^2-||z||^2-2t f_N; substitution of f_N gives (2.4).

## 3. H2 and off-root identities retained jointly

For an actual step x_v=x_u-l g_u, the two ordered rows are

 Q_uv=f_u-f_v+(1/2-l)||g_u||^2-<g_u,g_v>+||g_v||^2/2,
 Q_vu=f_v-f_u+||g_u||^2/2+(l-1)<g_u,g_v>+||g_v||^2/2.

Coefficient collection gives

 V_(l)=-2Q_uv-2(1+l)Q_vu+(2-l^2)||g_u-g_v||^2,          (3.1)
 ||g_u||^2-||g_v||^2
   =(2/l-1)||g_u-g_v||^2-(2/l)(Q_uv+Q_vu), l>0.         (3.2)

These identities keep both formal gradients.

For general finite nonnegative left and right words, define their capacities
P_L=1+sum(left) and P_R=1+sum(right). Insert the
actual bridge1+t, 0<=t<P_R. Let p be its query and z the parent endpoint;
j ranges over the nonterminal right queries with steps b_j. Define

 K=(2P_L+P_R+t)/(P_R-t),
 Delta=P_L P_R-t(P_L+P_R+t),
 R_prim=(P_L+t)Delta/(P_R-t),
 E=sum_j b_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)+t Q_(p,z)+sum_j b_j Q_(j,z).

On the actual history, without any primitive assumption, one has

    V_parent=V_left+K V_right-(K-1)E
                         +R_prim(||g_p||^2-||g_z||^2).       (3.3)

Here is a coefficient check of the off-root term. Expansion of E gives
value coefficients

    2sum_j b_j f_j+(t-P_R)f_p+(2-P_R-t)f_z

and gradient terms

 sum_j b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2
 +[(P_R-t)/2-t^2]||g_p||^2+(P_R+t)||g_z||^2/2.

All mixed terms cancel: with G=sum b_j g_j, the two sums contribute
respectively t<G,g_p>+||G||^2/2 and-||G||^2/2-<G,g_z>, in addition to
half their displayed diagonals; Q_(z,p) contributes t<g_z,g_p>+<G,g_z>,
and tQ_(p,z) contributes-t<g_p,G>-t<g_p,g_z>. The remaining value
coefficients match because(K-1)(P_R-t)=2(P_L+t). The unmatched gradient
coefficients are P_L^2-Kt^2=R_prim and K P_R^2-(P_L+P_R+t)^2=-R_prim. Factoring
after multiplication by P_R-t verifies these two identities. This proves
(3.3) without an exact-root substitution or a changed trajectory.

For H2 only, put C1=1+A=2+a and

 K1=(3C1+beta)/(C1-beta),
 Delta1=C1^2-beta(2C1+beta), R_prim1=(C1+beta)Delta1/(C1-beta),
 deltaA=2-A^2.

Let u=sqrt(2)-1. Then a=u(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon), 2u+u^2=1, and

 u C1-beta=epsilon(1+epsilon-2u^2)/(1+epsilon)>0,
 Delta1=(u C1-beta)(lambda C1+beta)>0.                (3.4)

Also1<A<sqrt(2),1<B<2,C1>beta, and K1>1. Apply (3.3) to the two
leaves, then (3.2) to bridge B and the final leaf A. Together with (3.1)
this gives the ENTIRE H2 identity, on its four original gradients,

 V_H2=-sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
       +e0||g0-g1||^2+e1||g1-g2||^2+e2||g2-g3||^2,   (3.5)

 e0=deltaA, e1=R_prim1(2/B-1)=R_prim1 epsilon/B,
 e2=K1 deltaA+R_prim1(2/A-1),

 Lambda_01=2,
 Lambda_10=2(1+A),
 Lambda_12=2R_prim1/B,
 Lambda_21=(K1-1)A+2R_prim1/B,
 Lambda_13=(K1-1)beta,
 Lambda_31=K1-1,
 Lambda_23=2K1+(K1-1)A+2R_prim1/A,
 Lambda_32=2K1(1+A)+2R_prim1/A.                           (3.6)

All other multipliers are zero; none is negative. The value coefficients
are(2A,2B,2A,-2s): for the bridge they give
-2A+(K1-1)(C1-beta)=2B, and at the last nonterminal point they give
-2(K1-1)A+2K1 A=2A. The square remainder is a positive weighted path
Laplacian on all four gradients. No formal coordinates are identified.

There is also the conservative bound

    e_i>=epsilon^2/2, i=0,1,2.                       (3.7)

To check it, deltaA=[2u epsilon/(1+epsilon)](sqrt(2)+A)>epsilon/2;
u>1/4, epsilon<1, and sqrt(2)+A>2 suffice. Formula (3.4), u<1/2,
and C1>2 give uC1-beta>epsilon/4 and lambda C1+beta>4. Hence
Delta1>epsilon and R_prim1>epsilon. Since B<2, (3.7) follows. These estimates follow from the displayed identities.

For the actual H3 root use C=1+s and k=a*c^2. Equation (3.3) reads

 K2=(3C+k)/(C-k), Delta2=C^2-k(2C+k),
 R_prim2=(C+k)Delta2/(C-k),
 V_H3=V_L+K2 V_R-(K2-1)E2+R_prim2(||g3||^2-||g7||^2).     (3.8)

E2 is the displayed E with p=3,z=7 and j=4,5,6. Its physical right
steps are A,B,A. Every row and the two H2 histories remain actual.

The sign is genuinely R_prim2<0. Indeed
C=5-epsilon+2u(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon), k=lambda(1-epsilon^2),
and lambda=5u+2u^2 give

 k-uC=epsilon[u+4u^2/(1+epsilon)-lambda epsilon]>0.

Here u>2/5, lambda<5/2 and epsilon<1/8 ensure the last bracket is
positive. Thus Delta2=(uC-k)(lambda C+k)<0. Primitive positivity is
not a premise of the H3 proof.

## 4. Every actual maximization remainder

For any actual word of mass t, input v, endpoint z and trailing d>=1,
write

 M=d+2t, q=P(z/d), p=P(v/M),
 bbar=[v-z+(d+t)q]/M,
 Rem(v,M,bbar)=M||p-bbar||^2+2<v-Mp,p-bbar>.           (4.1)

The point bbar belongs to D0: its physical gradient weights are the
actual nonnegative steps, and its q weight is d+t; their sum is M.
Each term in Rem is nonnegative by (2.1). Comparing the maximum at p
with the feasible point bbar, then expanding the squares, gives EXACTLY

 U_M(v)-U_d(z)
   =[||v||^2-||z+tq||^2]/M+Rem(v,M,bbar).             (4.2)

For instance v-M bbar=z-(d+t)q, which makes the expansion immediate:
the numerator after subtracting M U_d(z) is
||v||^2-||z||^2-2t<z,q>-t^2||q||^2.

Put g=P(z). Comparing the optimizers of U_d(z) at two different d values
gives, for1<=d1<d2 and q_i=P(z/d_i),

 d1(||q1||^2-||q2||^2)
 <=2<z,q1-q2><=d2(||q1||^2-||q2||^2).

It follows that the squared-norm and inner-product differences are
nonnegative. Consequently the exact radial term

 Lrad(t,z,d)=2t<z,g-q>+t^2(||g||^2-||q||^2)>=0         (4.3)

satisfies

 U_M(v)-U_d(z)=[V_word+Lrad(t,z,d)]/M+Rem(v,M,bbar).  (4.4)

This proves the H2 U law from (3.5), for ALL starts and d>=1. It also
retains the actual remainders, instead of assigning them values from a
different trajectory.

All remainders are themselves sums of original augmented normal rows
and one square. In (4.1), let bbar=sum theta_i g_i with theta_i>=0 and
sum theta_i=1, including the actual auxiliary q sample. The initial
auxiliary sample has input v/M and gradient p. Formula (2.3) gives

 Rem=M||p-bbar||^2-2M sum_i theta_i Q_(i,p).           (4.5)

Here Q_(i,p)=<v/M-p,g_i-p>. Thus no separate unproved positivity
principle is concealed in the notation Rem.

## 5. the primitive-left / actual-auxiliary bridge identity

Keep a general left word of mass s_L and
capacity C_L=1+s_L, with actual input x and endpoint v. Let a_p=P(v).
Apply the ACTUAL bridge1+k, k>=0, to obtain

 w=v-(1+k)a_p, h=C_L+k=s_L+1+k.

Let D>0 be an independent trailing parameter, and define

 M=D+2h, r=P(w/D), p0=P(x/M),
 zP=[x-w+(D+h)r]/M,
 Np=<v-a_p,a_p-r>, Nr=<w-D r,r-a_p>,
 Theta=h[C_L D-k(C_L+D+k)]/(D+k).                  (5.1)

Both Np and Nr are nonnegative. In augmented row notation,
Np=-Q_(r,p), Nr=-D Q_(p,r), where the r sample has input w/D and the
p sample is the physical bridge query v. Every point is actual.
The mixture zP uses all left physical gradients, the bridge gradient
with weight1+k, and r with weightD+h. These weights sum to M.

Without any assumed signs for the primitive energy V_L, one has

 U_M(x)-U_D(w)
   = [V_L+Theta||a_p-r||^2
        +2h(1+C_L/(D+k))Np+2h C_L/(D+k)Nr]/M
       +Rem(x,M,zP).                                (5.2)

Here is a full derivation. Put X=v+s_L a_p and e=a_p-r. Then

 w+h r=X-h e,
 ||x||^2-||w+h r||^2=V_L+2h<X,e>-h^2||e||^2.        (5.3)

The actual projection normals give the exact relations

 Np+Nr=(D+k)<a_p,e>-D||e||^2,
 <X,e>=C_L<a_p,e>+Np
       =C_L D||e||^2/(D+k)
          +(1+C_L/(D+k))Np+C_L Nr/(D+k).            (5.4)

Substitute (5.4) in (5.3) and then apply (4.2) to the prefix mass h
with trailing D. The square coefficient is
h[2C_L D/(D+k)-h]=Theta, proving (5.2).

Therefore, if

    V_L>=0 and C_L D>=k(C_L+D+k),                   (5.5)

then U_D(w)<=U_(D+2h)(x). This is a reusable quantified lemma with
independent C_L,D and actual physical bridge k. The numerical capacity
condition is not a substitute for the displayed actual left reserve.
A right word of mass s_R may be appended if its actual U law holds at
trailing d; use D=d+2s_R. No equality C_L=1+s_R is required by (5.2).

## 6. Application to both actual H2 children: nonnegative H3 identity

Now specialize to the actual H3. Write

 x=x0, v=x3, a_p=g3, w=x4, y=x7, b=g7,
 D=d+2s, M=d+2S=D+2h,
 q=P(y/d), r=P(w/D), p0=P(x/M),
 zR=[w-y+(d+s)q]/D,
 zP=[x-w+(D+h)r]/M,
 RR=Rem(w,D,zR), RP=Rem(x,M,zP),
 LR=Lrad(s,y,d).

The initial maximizer of the right child is EXACTLY r, the final
auxiliary maximizer in (5.2). There is no independent choice or reset.
Let V_L and V_R be the actual H2 energies (3.5), using respectively
indices0,1,2,3 and4,5,6,7.

The scalar coefficient is strictly positive for every d>=1. Since
beta>9/10, c<5/2, one has a>1/3, C=4+2a+beta>5, and
k=lambda(1-epsilon^2)<5/2<C/2. Also D>=2C-1. Hence

 C D-k(C+D+k)=(C-k)D-k(C+k)
   > (C/2)(2C-1)-3C^2/4=C(C-2)/4>0.                (6.1)

There is a uniform-in-d lower bound for the actual square coefficient:
D>9C/5 and k<C/2 imply k/(D+k)<5/23, so

 Theta/h=C-k-2Ck/(D+k)>C/2-10C/23=3C/46,
 Theta>3C(C+k)/46.                                 (6.2)

Summing (5.2) and the actual right-child identity (4.4) gives

 U_M(x)-U_d(y)
  = V_L/M+(V_R+LR)/D
    +[Theta||a_p-r||^2
       +2h(1+C/(D+k))Np+2h C/(D+k)Nr]/M
    +RP+RR.                                        (6.3)

Every term on the right is nonnegative after substituting the complete
H2 formula (3.5). Equations (3.5)-(3.6), (4.5), and the explicit two
normal rows after (5.1) make (6.3) a nonnegative original-row identity
on the physical and actual auxiliary samples of one fixed body.
In particular it retains the actual adjacent-gradient reserves

 epsilon^2/(2M) sum_(i=0)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2
 +epsilon^2/(2D) sum_(i=4)^6||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2,

as well as the root auxiliary square of coefficientTheta/M.
No claim is made that the adjacent squares alone pay the root defect.

This proves assertion (B).
At d=1, U1=2F; (2.2) also yields

    2F(T_H3 x)<=dist(x,K0)^2/(1+2S3).

This remains a projection-gradient-class assertion. No arbitrary-height
representation or full-function asymptotic conclusion is being used.



## Proof Part C: full seven-step value and sharpness

For a fixed body D0 containing0, its complete minimum set C_D0 is the polar cone proved above. For any selected minimum x_star in C_D0 and any d>0, every g in D0 satisfies x_star.g<=0. Therefore
 2x.g-d||g||^2 <=2(x-x_star).g-d||g||^2
 <=||x-x_star||^2/d
by completing the square. Taking the maximum gives U_d(x)<=||x-x_star||^2/d. Also U_1=2F_D0. Part B at d=1 now gives
 2F_D0(T_H3 x)<=||x-x_star||^2/(1+2S3).
This holds in particular for every origin-vertex simplex of the dimensions required by Part A. Taking its full restricted supremum and using Part A yields W_7(H_3)<=1/(1+2S3). This uses a fixed finite word for each fixed enclosing m and no interchange of a horizon limit and a supremum.

For the reverse inequality put S=S3 and tau=1/(1+2S). The one-dimensional function f(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=tau and f(x)=tau*|x|-tau^2/2 outside is globally convex, differentiable,1-smooth and uniquely minimized at0. Start at x0=1. Every partial sum of the nonnegative word lies in[0,S], so induction gives x_i=1-tau*sum_(j<i)h_j>=(1+S)/(1+2S)>=tau. Hence every used gradient equals tau, including the boundary case. At the actual seventh-step terminal,
 2f(x_7)=2tau-(2S+1)tau^2=tau.
The original selected radius is1, giving W_7(H_3)>=tau. Both inequalities prove(C). Multiplying each objective by L and dividing each physical step by L preserves the original W=2R normalization for general advertised L.

This finishes only(A),(B),(C). There is no asserted H_j U bound for all depths, no all-function asymptotic improvement, no uniform all-word minimax lower bound, and no original Goal completion.

## intuition
Strict-source lift and independent-coordinate perturbation preserve every projection normal, then compression and regularization prove pointwise finite-word supremum equality. An exact primitive-left/actual-auxiliary bridge identity and both actual H2 certificates prove the H3 U inequality. The polar-distance bound transfers it to the full class; a Huber instance attains equality.

  O_i: Sum d_i+d_{i+1} of the two ray offsets before stage i.
  Q_+: Radial derivative magnitude constructed on the nonnegative half-line.
  Q_-: Radial derivative magnitude constructed on the nonpositive half-line.
  a_i: Distance from0 of the starting point of stage i.
  b_i: Selected checkpoint step following block_i.

  d_i: Previous outermost distance on the active ray minus its derivative magnitude.
  e_term: Sum of the retained flat-segment areas s_i m_i^2 on earlier visits to the terminal ray.
  m_i: Positive gradient magnitude on nonterminal alternating-ray stage i.
  m_old: Previously attained derivative magnitude on the terminal ray, or0 if that ray has not been visited.
  q: Chosen final derivative magnitude in the displayed feasible interval.
  q_opt: The maximizing final derivative magnitude max(m_old,z_term/(2s_k+1)).
  r_i: Distance from0 of the endpoint of the new slope-1 ramp at nonterminal stage i.
  s_i: Sum of the ordinary steps in block_i.
  w_+: Derivative magnitude at the current outermost positive-ray record.
  w_-: Derivative magnitude at the current outermost negative-ray record.
  w_old: Derivative magnitude at A_old on the ray about to be extended.
  z_term: Terminal distance budget a_k-d_k.

## statement
Exact alternating-ray multi-cut hard functions with retained remote offsets and function-value areas.

Fix any integer n>=0, L>0, and any predetermined finite nonnegative schedule h of length n for gradient descent x_{t+1}=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). Write W_n(h) for the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional convex differentiable functions with L-Lipschitz gradient, a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star.

Choose k checkpoint steps, where 0<=k<=n, and write h in its exact chronological order as (block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,b_{k-1},block_k). Each block_i is a possibly empty list of nonnegative steps; s_i is its sum. Each b_i>=0 is the intervening checkpoint.

For k>=1 set m_0=1/(1+s_0) and O_0=0. For i=0,...,k-2 define
m_{i+1}=((b_i-1)m_i-O_i)/(1+s_{i+1}), O_{i+1}=O_i+s_i m_i.
Require m_i>0 for 0<=i<k and m_i>=m_{i-2} for 2<=i<k.
Define z_term=(b_{k-1}-1)m_{k-1}-O_{k-1}, and define m_old=0 if k=1 and m_old=m_{k-2} if k>=2. Require z_term>=(1+s_k)m_old.
Define e_term=sum_{0<=i<k, i congruent k mod 2}s_i m_i^2.
Choose any q in the closed interval [m_old,z_term/(1+s_k)].

Then an explicitly constructed one-dimensional convex differentiable function f with L-Lipschitz derivative, f(0)=0=min f, and initial point x_0=1 realizes the full supplied GD schedule and has exactly
2 f(x_n)/L = 2e_term+2q z_term-(2s_k+1)q^2.
Consequently this expression is a lower bound for W_n(h). The largest value within the stated q interval is attained at
q_opt=max{m_old,z_term/(2s_k+1)}.

For k=0, use z_term=1, e_term=0, m_old=0, s_k=s_0 and q in [0,1/(1+s_0)], without defining any m_i or O_i. The same construction and payoff formula apply and their maximum is 1/(1+2s_0). The empty horizon n=0 is included.
For k=1 the feasibility condition is exactly b_0>=1, and the optimized payoff is (b_0-1)^2/[(1+s_0)^2(1+2s_1)].

Define F_ray_n(h) as the maximum of the optimized displayed payoff over all feasible checkpoint choices, including k=0. This is a finite maximum because h is finite. Then
K_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h)<=W_n(h),
where K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2sum_t h_t), max_{0<=j<n}(h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+sum_{t<j}h_t)^2(1+2sum_{t>j}h_t)]} is the lower-bound functional established in predecessor b98bb807d514e80d, with its inner maximum omitted at n=0 and (u)_+=max(u,0).
For h=(1,5,1,9,1), the checkpoint choice (5,9) has exact optimized payoff77/4, whereas K_5(h)=1/3.
No uniform comparison between F_ray_n and the Zhang–Jiang profile is asserted.

## proof
## Explicit construction on the two rays

Write a signed point as x=(-1)^i r at stage i, where r>=0 is its distance
from0. On each ray construct a radial derivative magnitude Q_+(r) or Q_-(r),
starting at Q_+(0)=Q_-(0)=0. Let A_+ and A_- denote the largest distances
previously visited on those rays, and let w_+,w_- be their derivative
magnitudes. Initially all four numbers are0.

At a nonterminal stage i the starting distance is a_i. On its active ray
write A_old,w_old for the previous record and slope and d_i=A_old-w_old.
Choose the magnitude m_i and extend that ray's derivative by a slope-1 ramp
from (A_old,w_old) to

\[
(r_i,m_i),\qquad r_i=A_{\rm old}+m_i-w_{\rm old}=d_i+m_i,
\]

followed by a constant segment of value m_i up to a_i. The same-ray magnitude assumptions
give m_i>=w_old; for the first visit to a ray its old slope is0. The
recurrence will give

\[
a_i=d_i+(1+s_i)m_i=r_i+s_i m_i,
\tag{7}
\]

so a_i>=r_i>=A_old. This extension does not change any previously visited
data. During block_i every iterate remains on this flat segment, since its
partial step sums lie between0 and s_i. Thus the stage ends at signed
distance r_i with gradient sign (-1)^i and magnitude m_i.

At the checkpoint b_i the next signed point has opposite sign and distance

\[
a_{i+1}=b_i m_i-r_i=(b_i-1)m_i-d_i.
\tag{8}
\]

The previously unvisited amount will be nonnegative by the next-stage
feasibility condition. The active ray's new outer record is a_i and its
slope there is m_i. Hence its offset on its next visit is

\[
d_{i+2}=a_i-m_i=d_i+s_i m_i.
\tag{9}
\]

Initially d_0=d_1=0. Write O_i=d_i+d_{i+1}. Equations (8)--(9) imply
O_{i+1}=O_i+s_i m_i and
a_{i+1}-d_{i+1}=(b_i-1)m_i-O_i. Equating this last quantity with
(1+s_{i+1})m_{i+1} gives exactly the recurrence in the statement. Also a_0=1 and d_0=0 imply the
stated m_0, completing the induction that verifies (7) for each nonterminal
stage. Positive m_i means nonterminal stages do not reach0. The terminal
case z_term=0 is permitted only with zero old slope and causes no difficulty.

At terminal stage k, the starting distance satisfies a_k-d_k=z_term. Extend
its ray by a slope-1 ramp from its old record to slope q and then by a flat
segment to a_k. The assumed feasible interval for q makes the ramp endpoint d_k+q no smaller
than the old record and no larger than a_k-s_k q. Thus every final-block
iterate stays on its flat segment. The final distance is a_k-s_k q.

For k=0 the same construction begins directly from the initial origin datum,
with a_0=1 and q in its stated feasible interval.

After the last stage extend each radial derivative constantly to infinity.
Define the global derivative by f'(x)=Q_+(x) for x>=0 and
f'(x)=-Q_-(-x) for x<=0, and define f(x)=integral_0^x f'(t)dt.
Each radial derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz, with
Q_+(0)=Q_-(0)=0. Across0, for x<=0<=y,

\[
0\leq f'(y)-f'(x)=Q_+(y)+Q_-(-x)\leq y-x.
\]

Consequently f' is globally continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz.
This proves differentiability, convexity, 1-smoothness, and a minimizer at0.
The earlier stage calculations prove the exact original GD recursion.
In particular no interpolation or dimensional-realizability gap remains
within the candidate construction.

## Retained function values give the exact objective

On one ray, a ramp from magnitude w to v adds area (v^2-w^2)/2. Its
following nonterminal flat segment has length s_i m_i and adds s_i m_i^2.
Therefore at the outer record after any nonterminal stage i, the radial
function value equals

\[
m_i^2/2+\sum_{j\leq i,\ j\equiv i\ ({\rm mod}\ 2)}s_jv_j^2.
\tag{10}
\]

This identity follows by induction on successive visits to that ray: the
squared-gradient contributions telescope and the flat-segment areas remain.
At the final stage the new ramp endpoint has value e_term+q^2/2. The distance
from that endpoint to the final point is z_term-(s_k+1)q, on which the derivative
equals q. The final value is consequently

\[
e_term+q^2/2+q[z_term-(s_k+1)q]
=e_term+qZ-(s_k+1/2)q^2,
\]

proving the stated exact payoff. The initial distance is exactly1, so the normalization is exact.

## Exact two-cut illustration

For h=(1,5,1,9,1) select checkpoint steps5 and9. Then s_0=s_1=s_2=1,
m_0=1/2, O_0=0, m_1=1, O_1=1/2, z_term=15/2, m_old=1/2,
e_term=1/4, and q_opt=5/2. All feasibility conditions hold. The exact payoff formula gives

\[
W_5(1,5,1,9,1)\geq2(1/4)+(15/2)^2/3=77/4.
\]

The actual points are 1,1/2,-2,-1,8,11/2. On the positive ray the
derivative is r on [0,1/2], 1/2 on [1/2,1], r-1/2 on [1,3], and5/2
on [3,infinity). On the negative ray its magnitude is min(r,1).
Direct integration gives f(11/2)=77/8, consistent with the formula.
By contrast the exact K_5 value is max{1/35,4/23,1/3}=1/3.



Optimization, base cases, and lower-bound conclusion.
For any s_k>=0 the function q -> 2e_term+2q z_term-(2s_k+1)q^2 is a strictly concave quadratic. Its unconstrained maximum is z_term/(2s_k+1). Feasibility implies z_term>=0, and 2s_k+1>=s_k+1, so that maximum does not exceed the interval's upper endpoint. Clamping only at its lower endpoint gives the stated q_opt. When k=0, the construction is a ramp from0 to q followed by a plateau to the initial point1. Each ordinary step stays in that plateau because q<=1/(1+s_0). The objective formula is 2q-(2s_0+1)q^2 and its optimum is1/(2s_0+1), including s_0=0 and n=0. When k=1, z_term=(b_0-1)/(1+s_0), m_old=0 and e_term=0. Feasibility is b_0>=1, and optimizing gives z_term^2/(2s_1+1). The case b_0=1, q=0 reaches0 at the checkpoint and remains there; the negative ray can have identically zero derivative, so this is a legal degenerate case. Checkpoints with b_0<1 contribute only a zero term to K_n and need not be selected. Thus k=0 and k=1 recover all terms of K_n as defined in predecessor fact b98bb807d514e80d. There are at most2^n selected checkpoint sets, so the maximum defining F_ray_n is finite and nonempty. Each feasible choice is realized by a legal function and has the exact computed payoff, hence their maximum is at most W_n(h) and is at least K_n(h). This finite counting observation is not a numerical enumeration or a computational ingredient in the proof.

Every construction above was at L=1. For general L>0 replace the constructed function f_1 by f_L=L f_1. Its derivative is L-Lipschitz and its GD update with step h_t/L is exactly the same as f_1's update with step h_t. Its minimizer0, initial point1, and dimension one stay the same. The normalized objective 2f_L(x_n)/L equals2f_1(x_n), proving the stated normalization for all L. Zero ordinary steps repeat a point and cause no change in the proof. All step and derived parameter values are finite real numbers; every denominator s_i+1 or2s_k+1 is positive. Arbitrarily large isolated or clustered checkpoint steps are allowed whenever the explicitly stated feasibility conditions hold. No claim is made that an arbitrary checkpoint selection satisfies those conditions.

## intuition
The function is built only beyond previously visited distances on alternating rays. Exact offsets prevent illegal overwriting of old interpolation data, and the accumulated flat-segment areas preserve old function values. This yields a genuine multi-cut lower bound extending the single-cut Huber construction, without asserting the missing uniform profile comparison.
